# PASS 2 on commit `79cd12e` — the receipt that round did not file

## READ THIS FIRST — PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY

A recent panel had seven of nine seats confirm a premise this brief's author stated wrongly.
**Check the premises, not just the derivations.** Where I state something as fact, ask whether it is
true. A seat that re-derives a premise from something this brief did NOT give it is worth more than
a seat that agrees.

**THE TREE UNDER REVIEW IS `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`.** There are THREE pristine-2021-upstream copies of
GXemul under the project root (`./src/` and two under `_archive/audit-results-*/builds/`). They are
NOT this fork. If your line numbers disagree with mine by hundreds you are reading one of them — say
so rather than reconciling.

**If you cannot read files that is expected** — the product diff is inlined in full below. Name any
premise you cannot settle from this packet rather than guessing at it.

**Where this brief states an ARITHMETIC claim, ENUMERATE it rather than reasoning about it.**

**VERDICT LINE FIRST**, so your answer survives truncation.

## 1. WHY THIS PASS IS HAPPENING AT ALL — and it is not a formality

`precommit_check.sh` section F refuses the next commit with:

    FAIL  NO PASS-2 RECEIPT for 79cd12e #502 pass 9-12 and #503
    FAIL  A round owes a pass 2 on its own diff.  R6 and R7 both shipped without one,
    FAIL  the second time one round after the first was caught and written down.

So this is a **retrospective** pass on shipped code. The late-dissent rule governs what your findings
can do:

* **WRONG RECORD → L4**, corrected in place. Closure is no shield for false prose.
* **MEASURED FALSE PASS** against the detector's floor, contract or obligations → **L2/L3**, fixed in
  a follow-up commit.
* **EVERYTHING ELSE, design dissents included → FILED as a new queue item**, carrying the dissent
  verbatim.

**Closure changes the LICENSE, never the truth-value.** Do not soften a finding because the code
already shipped — say what is true and I will route it.

a follow-up — that is a better outcome than you guessing.

## 3. THE TWO FIXES, as the round described them

**`#503 lunaabsentcpu` (`dev_luna88k.c`).** `reassert_interrupts()` walked a fixed CPU count and
called through a NULL interrupt pointer for CPUs the machine does not have. The fix captures
`d->ncpus` at DEVINIT and bounds the walk.

**`#502 m88kexcdeliver` (`cpu_m88k.c`).** The m88k exception core stopped EVERY guest CPU for a fault
raised on one. The round ran **twelve passes**; passes 9–12 closed a cell space the round's own
contract had disclosed.

**`dev_wdc.c`** carries an L4 records correction only — a census comment qualified to say it counts
helper CALLS, not successful pops.

## 4. THE QUESTIONS

### Q1 — THE PREMISE AUDIT, on the fix itself

For each of the two fixes: **is the guard right, and is it in the right place?** Specifically —

1. `#503` captures `ncpus` at **DEVINIT**. Is that value stable for the life of the device, or can
   the machine's CPU count change after init? If it can, the capture is a snapshot of a moving value
   and the bound is wrong.
2. `#502`'s repair closes cells in a state space. **Does the diff below actually close what its
   comments claim**, or does it close the cell the mutant used and leave the symmetric one open?
   That exact shape — *the original breach reproduced inside its own repair* — is what `#499` was
   caught doing, so it is worth checking rather than assuming.

### Q2 — THE SMALLEST EDIT

**What is the smallest edit to either fix that re-opens the defect and still passes its detector?**
Name the row you expect to stay green. This is the highest-yield question in the project's record and
it is the reason a retrospective pass is worth firing at all.

### Q3 — THE FLOOR, asked of the SHIPPED COMPOSITE

Per the repair-chain rule, review EFFORT is scoped to this diff, but the floor question is asked of
the shipped detector as a whole: **does a clean-sweep mutant exist in the clause-(d) DEPENDS-ON
enumeration** — the predicates dominating the edited lines, walked to the enclosing function
boundary, plus operand swaps drawn from fields the guarded body reads?

**If you find one, that is an L2 floor breach and it gets repaired**, however late. If you find none,
say so **with your denominator** — which cells, which mutant shapes you swept. *"Zero found"* and
*"zero looked for"* must not render the same.

### Q4 — WHAT DID THE ROUND'S OWN TWELVE PASSES MISS?

`#502` ran twelve passes and each of the first several found a real defect. A round that converges
after twelve passes has either genuinely converged or stopped looking. **You have the diff and none
of the fatigue.**

**If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE — say so.** Tonight's best findings arrived
that way, including two defects in code everyone had called correct.

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

  645: /*
  646:  *  m88k_memory_transaction_debug_dump():
  647:  *
  648:  *  Debug dump of the memory transaction registers of a cpu.
  649:  */
  650: static void m88k_memory_transaction_debug_dump(struct cpu *cpu, int n)
  651: {
  652: 	uint32_t dmt = cpu->cd.m88k.dmt[n];
  653: 
  654: 	debug("[ DMT%i: ", n);
  655: 	if (dmt & DMT_VALID) {
  656: 		if (dmt & DMT_BO)
  657: 			debug("Little-Endian, ");
  658: 		else
  659: 			debug("Big-Endian, ");
  660: 		if (dmt & DMT_DAS)
  661: 			debug("Supervisor, ");
  662: 		else
  663: 			debug("User, ");
  664: 		if (dmt & DMT_DOUB1)
  665: 			debug("DOUB1, ");
  666: 		if (dmt & DMT_LOCKBAR)
  667: 			debug("LOCKBAR, ");
  668: 		if (dmt & DMT_WRITE)
  669: 			debug("store, ");
  670: 		else {
  671: 			debug("load.%c(r%i), ",
  672: 			    dmt & DMT_SIGNED? 's' : 'u',
  673: 			    DMT_DREGBITS(dmt));
  674: 		}
  675: 		debug("bytebits=0x%x ]\n", DMT_ENBITS(dmt));
  676: 
  677: 		debug("[ DMD%i: 0x%08" PRIx32"; ", n, cpu->cd.m88k.dmd[n]);
  678: 		debug("DMA%i: 0x%08" PRIx32" ]\n", n, cpu->cd.m88k.dma[n]);
  679: 	} else
  680: 		debug("not valid ]\n");
  681: }

  684: /*
  685:  *  m88k_exception():
  686:  *
  687:  *  Cause an exception.
  688:  */
  689: void m88k_exception(struct cpu *cpu, int vector, int is_trap)
  690: {
  691: 	bool update_shadow_regs = true;
  692: 	bool implemented = false;
  693: 
  694: 	// TODO: Use a const char * instead or something like that, inside the switch...
  695: 
  696: 	switch (vector) {
  697: 
  698: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_RESET:
  699: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "RESET", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  700: 		implemented = true;
  701: 		break;
  702: 
  703: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_INTERRUPT:
  704: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "INTERRUPT", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  705: 		implemented = true;
  706: 		break;
  707: 
  708: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_INSTRUCTION_ACCESS:
  709: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "INSTRUCTION_ACCESS", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  710: 		implemented = true;
  711: 		break;
  712: 
  713: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_DATA_ACCESS:
  714: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "DATA_ACCESS", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  715: 		implemented = true;
  716: 		break;
  717: 
  718: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_MISALIGNED_ACCESS:
  719: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "MISALIGNED_ACCESS", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  720: 		implemented = true;	/*  m88kexcdeliver  */
  721: 		break;
  722: 
  723: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE:
  724: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  725: 		implemented = true;	/*  m88kexcdeliver  */
  726: 		break;
  727: 
  728: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION:
  729: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  730: 		implemented = true;	/*  m88kexcdeliver  */
  731: 		break;
  732: 
  733: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_BOUNDS_CHECK_VIOLATION:
  734: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "BOUNDS_CHECK_VIOLATION", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  735: 		implemented = true;	/*  m88kexcdeliver  */
  736: 		break;
  737: 
  738: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE:
  739: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  740: 		implemented = true;	/*  m88kexcdeliver  */
  741: 		break;
  742: 
  743: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_INTEGER_OVERFLOW:
  744: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "INTEGER_OVERFLOW", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  745: 		implemented = true;	/*  m88kexcdeliver  */
  746: 		break;
  747: 
  748: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_ERROR:
  749: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "ERROR", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  750: 		implemented = true;	/*  m88kexcdeliver  */
  751: 		break;
  752: 
  753: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_SFU1_PRECISE:
  754: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "SFU1_PRECISE", VERBOSITY_ERROR, "");
  755: 		break;
  756: 
  757: 	case M88K_EXCEPTION_SFU1_IMPRECISE:
  758: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "SFU1_IMPRECISE", VERBOSITY_ERROR, "");
  759: 		break;
  760: 
  761: 	case 0x80:	/*  up to OpenBSD 5.2  */
  762: 	case 0x1c2:	/*  from OpenBSD 5.3 and forward...  */
  763: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "syscall", VERBOSITY_INFO,
  764: 		    "r13=%i", cpu->cd.m88k.r[13]);
  765: #if 0
  766: 		fatal("[ syscall %i(", cpu->cd.m88k.r[13]);
  767: 		m88k_cpu_functioncall_trace(cpu, 8);
  768: 		fatal(") ]\n");
  769: #endif
  770: 		break;
  771: 
  772: 	case MVMEPROM_VECTOR:
  773: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "MVMEPROM_VECTOR", VERBOSITY_INFO, "");
  774: 		break;
  775: 
  776: 	default:
  777: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "unknown vector",
  778: 		    vector < M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START ? VERBOSITY_ERROR
  779: 		    : VERBOSITY_INFO, "0x%03x", vector);
  780: 	}
  781: 
  782: 	/*
  783: 	 *  m88kexcdeliver (2026-09-05): the seven trap-like classes above are
  784: 	 *  DELIVERED (VBR + 8*vector, SXIP = the faulting instruction, SNIP = the
  785: 	 *  next one, destination register unchanged -- MC88100 UM 6.4.3/6.5/6.6/
  786: 	 *  6.8.3), like the four this fallback never covered.  Before, any of them
  787: 	 *  stopped EVERY guest CPU here: one divu by zero, in either mode, ended
  788: 	 *  the emulation (measured: regress/m88k_exc_probe.py rows div0/udiv0).
  789: 	 *  Pre-existing upstream code (this fallback is the 0.7.0 import),
  790: 	 *  surfaced by the 2026-09-05 retrospective of #380.  What still lands
  791: 	 *  here, by design: SFU1_PRECISE/IMPRECISE (114/115; row m88kexcsfu1 --
  792: 	 *  faithful delivery needs the rest of the SFU1 exception state, which
  793: 	 *  the fork does not write) and vectors 11..113/116..127 (117 vectors
  794: 	 *  with the two SFU1 ones), which no literal-constant raise site names
  795: 	 *  but a supervisor tb0/tb1/tcnd can name directly (row
  796: 	 *  m88kexctaildefault; the probe's resid50/sfu1h rows pin that they
  797: 	 *  STILL halt).
  798: 	 */
  799: 	if (vector < M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START && !implemented) {
  800: 		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "unimplemented",
  801: 		    VERBOSITY_ERROR, "vector=0x%03x pc=0x%08x",
  802: 			vector,
  803: 			(int)cpu->pc);
  804: 		for (int j = 0; j < cpu->machine->ncpus; ++j)
  805: 			cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = false;
  806: 		m88k_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
  807: 		return;
  808: 	}
  809: 
  810: 	/*  Stuff common for all exceptions:  */
  811: 	if (cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_PSR] & M88K_PSR_SFRZ) {
  812: 		/*
  813: 		 *  Non-trap exceptions when the shadow freeze bit is already
  814: 		 *  set result in an Error exception:
  815: 		 */
  816: 		if (!is_trap) {
  819: 		}
  828: 	    | M88K_PSR_SFRZ	/*  Freeze shadow registers,          */  831: 	    | M88K_PSR_MODE,	/*  and switch to supervisor mode.    */
  832: 	    M88K_CR_PSR, 0);
  834: 	if (update_shadow_regs) {  837: 		cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SXIP] = cpu->pc | M88K_XIP_V;
  838: 
  839: 		/*  SNIP is the address to return to, when executing rte:  */
  840: 		if (cpu->delay_slot) {
  841: 			if (vector == M88K_EXCEPTION_DATA_ACCESS) {
  842: 				cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] = cpu->cd.m88k.delay_target | M88K_NIP_V;
  843: 				cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP]+4;
  844: 			} else if (vector == M88K_EXCEPTION_INSTRUCTION_ACCESS) {
  845: 				/*  If we are in a delay slot, then pc is
  846: 				    something like 0xc6000 here (not 0xc5ffc).  */
  847: 				cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] = cpu->cd.m88k.delay_target | M88K_NIP_V;
  848: 				cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] = 0;
  849: 			} else {
  850: 				/*  Perhaps something like this could work:  */
  851: 				cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] = (cpu->pc + 4) | M88K_NIP_V;
  852: 				cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] = cpu->cd.m88k.delay_target | M88K_NIP_V;
  853: 			}
  854: 		} else {
  855: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] = (cpu->pc + 4) | M88K_NIP_V;
  856: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP]+4;
  857: 		}
  858: 
  859: 		if (vector == M88K_EXCEPTION_INSTRUCTION_ACCESS)
  860: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SXIP] |= M88K_XIP_E;
  861: 	}
  862: 
  863: 	cpu->pc = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_VBR] + 8 * vector;
  864: 
  865: 	if (cpu->delay_slot)
  866: 		cpu->delay_slot = EXCEPTION_IN_DELAY_SLOT;
  867: 	else
  868: 		cpu->delay_slot = NOT_DELAYED;
  869: 
  870: 	/*  Default to no memory transactions:  */
  871: 	cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMT0] = 0;
  872: 	cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMD0] = 0;
  873: 	cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMA0] = 0;
  874: 	cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMT1] = 0;
  875: 	cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMD1] = 0;
  876: 	cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMA1] = 0;
  877: 	cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMT2] = 0;
  878: 	cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMD2] = 0;
  879: 	cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMA2] = 0;
  880: 
  881: 	/*  Vector-specific handling:  */
  882: 	if (vector < M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START) {
  883: 		switch (vector) {
  884: 
  885: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_RESET:
  886: 			fatal("[ m88k_exception: reset ]\n");
  887: 			for (int j = 0; j < cpu->machine->ncpus; ++j)
  888: 				cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = false;
  889: 			break;
  890: 
  891: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_INTERRUPT:
  892: 			/*  When returning with rte, we want to re-  */
  893: 			/*  execute the interrupted instruction:  */
  894: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] -= 4;
  895: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] -= 4;
  896: 			break;
  897: 
  898: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_INSTRUCTION_ACCESS:
  899: 			/*  When returning with rte, we want to re-  */
  900: 			/*  execute the instruction in SXIP, not SNIP/SFIP,  */
  901: 			/*  (unless the exception was in a delay-slot):  */
  902: 			if (!(cpu->delay_slot & EXCEPTION_IN_DELAY_SLOT)) {
  903: 				cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] = 0;
  904: 				cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] = 0;
  905: 			}
  906: 			break;
  907: 
  908: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_DATA_ACCESS:
  909: 			/*  Update the memory transaction registers:  */
  910: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMT0] = cpu->cd.m88k.dmt[0];
  911: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMD0] = cpu->cd.m88k.dmd[0];
  912: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMA0] = cpu->cd.m88k.dma[0];
  913: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMT1] = cpu->cd.m88k.dmt[1];
  914: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMD1] = cpu->cd.m88k.dmd[1];
  915: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMA1] = cpu->cd.m88k.dma[1];
  916: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMT2] = 0;
  917: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMD2] = 0;
  918: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_DMA2] = 0;
  919: 			m88k_memory_transaction_debug_dump(cpu, 0);
  920: 			m88k_memory_transaction_debug_dump(cpu, 1);
  921: 			break;
  922: 
  923: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_MISALIGNED_ACCESS:
  924: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE:
  925: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION:
  926: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_BOUNDS_CHECK_VIOLATION:
  927: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE:
  928: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_INTEGER_OVERFLOW:
  929: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_ERROR:
  930: 			/*  m88kexcdeliver: OUTSIDE A DELAY SLOT the common path above
  931: 			    is already the architectural contract for these -- SXIP =
  932: 			    the faulting instruction, SNIP = the next one, SFIP = SNIP+4,
  933: 			    and rte resumes at SNIP (UM 6.4.3.1.4: "the processor
  934: 			    hardware does not use the SXIP on return from an exception"),
  935: 			    so the upstream doubt that sat here in an #if 0 ("should the
  936: 			    PC be backed up one step?") is answered NO: 6.5.x/6.8.3 say
  937: 			    the destination register is unchanged (6.6: and the carry
  938: 			    bit, for the overflow case), and a handler that wants
  939: 			    re-execution arranges SNIP/SFIP itself.  INSIDE a delay slot
  940: 			    the else-arm above ("Perhaps something like this could
  941: 			    work") is upstream's guess and the manual disagrees (Table
  942: 			    6-3: the shadows copy XIP/NIP/FIP, and UM 6.2 defines FIP as
  943: 			    the taken branch's target while the branch executes, so for
  944: 			    a fault in its delay slot NIP = target, FIP = target+4) --
  945: 			    pre-existing, now reachable by ordinary faults, row
  946: 			    m88kdslotshadow.
  947: 			    Without these labels the classes reached the default: below,
  948: 			    which stops the cpu -- the one-site fix was measured to do
  949: 			    exactly that; with them, nothing below 128 reaches that
  950: 			    default any more (it is unreachable code kept as the
  951: 			    tripwire for an eighth class).  ERROR arrives here by TWO
  952: 			    routes: the SFRZ re-route above (a fault inside a handler,
  953: 			    shadows deliberately frozen at the FIRST fault's values --
  954: 			    SXIP then names that first fault, not this one) and a
  955: 			    supervisor tb0/tb1/tcnd naming vector 10 directly (shadows
  956: 			    updated normally when that trap is taken UNFROZEN; inside a
  957: 			    handler it takes the is_trap exemption above and arrives
  958: 			    here with the shadows frozen).  Not modelled, disclosed: scoreboard
  959: 			    capture (the shadow-update path writes SSBR = 0; a frozen
  960: 			    SSBR keeps its old value) and fcr2/fcr4/fcr5 for the divide.  */
  961: 			break;
  962: 
  963: #if 0
  964: 		// TODO: Read up more about SFU1 exceptions and how the
  965: 		// control registers look when they happen.
  966: 		case M88K_EXCEPTION_SFU1_PRECISE:
  967: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] = 0; //?
  968: 			cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] = 0;
  969: 			//cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] -= 4;
  970: 			//cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] -= 4;
  971: 			break;
  972: #endif
  973: 
  974: 		default:debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "m88k",
  975: 			    VERBOSITY_ERROR, "internal error");
  976: 			cpu->running = false;
  977: 		}
  978: 	}
  979: 
  980: 	m88k_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
  981: }
  982: 
  983: 
  984: /*
  985:  *  m88k_cpu_disassemble_instr():
  986:  *
  987:  *  Convert an instruction word into human readable format, for instruction
  988:  *  tracing.
  989:  *              
  990:  *  If running is 1, cpu->pc should be the address of the instruction.
  991:  *
  992:  *  If running is 0, things that depend on the runtime environment (eg.
  993:  *  register contents) will not be shown, and dumpaddr will be used instead of
  994:  *  cpu->pc for relative addresses.
  995:  */                     
  996: int m88k_cpu_disassemble_instr(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *ib,
  997:         int running, uint64_t dumpaddr)
  998: {
  999: 	int supervisor = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_PSR] & M88K_PSR_MODE;
 1000: 	uint32_t iw;
 1001: 	const char *symbol, *mnem = NULL;
 1002: 	uint64_t offset;
 1003: 	uint32_t op26, op10, op11, d, s1, s2, w5, cr6, imm16;
 1004: 	int32_t d16, d26;
 1005: 
 1006: 	if (running)
 1007: 		dumpaddr = cpu->pc;
 1008: 
 1009: 	symbol = get_symbol_name(&cpu->machine->symbol_context,
 1010: 	    dumpaddr, &offset);
 1011: 	if (symbol != NULL && offset == 0 && supervisor)
 1012: 		debug("<%s>\n", symbol);
 1013: 
 1014: 	if (cpu->machine->ncpus > 1 && running)
 1015: 		debug("cpu%i:\t", cpu->cpu_id);
 1016: 
 1017: 	debug("%c%08" PRIx32": ",
 1018: 	    cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_PSR] & M88K_PSR_MODE? 's' : 'u',
 1019: 	    (uint32_t) dumpaddr);
 1020: 
 1021: 	if (cpu->byte_order == EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN)
 1022: 		iw = ib[0] + (ib[1]<<8) + (ib[2]<<16) + ((uint32_t)ib[3]<<24);
 1023: 	else
 1024: 		iw = ib[3] + (ib[2]<<8) + (ib[1]<<16) + ((uint32_t)ib[0]<<24);
 1025: 
 1026: 	debug("%08" PRIx32, (uint32_t) iw);
 1027: 
 1028: 	if (running && cpu->delay_slot)
 1029: 		debug(" (d)");
 1030: 	else
 1031: 		debug("    ");
 1032: 
 1033: 	cpu_print_pc_indicator_in_disassembly(cpu, running, dumpaddr);
 1034: 
 1035: 	op26   = (iw >> 26) & 0x3f;
 1036: 	op11   = (iw >> 11) & 0x1f;
 1037: 	op10   = (iw >> 10) & 0x3f;
 1038: 	d      = (iw >> 21) & 0x1f;
 1039: 	s1     = (iw >> 16) & 0x1f;
 1040: 	s2     =  iw        & 0x1f;
 1041: 	imm16  = iw & 0xffff;
 1042: 	w5     = (iw >>  5) & 0x1f;
 1043: 	cr6    = (iw >>  5) & 0x3f;
 1044: 	d16    = ((int16_t) (iw & 0xffff)) * 4;
 1045: 	d26    = ((int32_t)((iw & 0x03ffffff) << 6)) >> 4;
 1046: 
 1047: 	switch (op26) {
 1048: 
 1049: 	case 0x00:	/*  xmem.bu  */
 1050: 	case 0x01:	/*  xmem     */
 1051: 	case 0x02:	/*  ld.hu    */
 1052: 	case 0x03:	/*  ld.bu    */
 1053: 	case 0x04:	/*  ld.d     */
 1054: 	case 0x05:	/*  ld       */
 1055: 	case 0x06:	/*  ld.h     */
 1056: 	case 0x07:	/*  ld.b     */
 1057: 	case 0x08:	/*  st.d     */
 1058: 	case 0x09:	/*  st       */
 1059: 	case 0x0a:	/*  st.h     */
 1060: 	case 0x0b:	/*  st.b     */
 1061: 		if (iw == 0x00000000) {
 1062: 			debug("-\n");
 1063: 			break;
 1064: 		}
 1065: 		switch (op26) {
 1066: 		case 0x00:  debug("xmem.bu"); break;
 1067: 		case 0x01:  debug("xmem"); break;
 1068: 		case 0x02:  debug("ld.hu"); break;
 1069: 		case 0x03:  debug("ld.bu"); break;
 1070: 		default:    debug("%s%s", op26 >= 0x08? "st" : "ld",
 1071: 				memop[op26 & 3]);
 1072: 		}
 1073: 		debug("\tr%i,r%i,0x%x", d, s1, imm16);
 1074: 		if (running) {
 1075: 			uint32_t tmpaddr = cpu->cd.m88k.r[s1] + imm16;
 1076: 			symbol = get_symbol_name(&cpu->machine->symbol_context,
 1077: 			    tmpaddr, &offset);
 1078: 			if (symbol != NULL && supervisor)
 1079: 				debug("\t; [<%s>]", symbol);
 1080: 			else
src/devices/dev_luna88k.c
   80: #define	LUNA88K_REGISTERS_LENGTH	(LUNA88K_REGISTERS_END - LUNA88K_REGISTERS_BASE)
   81: 
   82: #define	MAX_CPUS	4
   83: 
   84: #define	SIO_QUEUE_SIZE	256
   85: 
   86: #define	BCD(x) ((((x) / 10) << 4) + ((x) % 10))
   87: 
   88: 
   89: struct luna88k_data {
   90: 	/*  #503 (lunaabsentcpu): the number of CPUs actually INTERRUPT_CONNECTed at
   91: 	    DEVINIT.  Slots at or above this index were never connected, so their
   92: 	    cpu_irq[].interrupt_assert/deassert pointers are the memset's NULL.  The
   93: 	    walk bound and the connect predicate carry the same VALUE -- not one
   94: 	    shared field: the connect predicates read machine->ncpus directly while
   95: 	    the walk reads this snapshot.  The equality holds because nothing in
   96: 	    this fork mutates the count after device init (pass 2, codex).  */
   97: 	int		ncpus;
   98: 	struct interrupt cpu_irq[MAX_CPUS];
   99: 	int		irqActive[MAX_CPUS];
  100: 	uint32_t	interrupt_enable[MAX_CPUS];
  101: 	uint32_t	interrupt_status;
  102: 	uint32_t	software_interrupt_status[MAX_CPUS];
  103: 	/*  #438: latched PER CPU, not per instance -- a per-instance latch
  104: 	    demotes the second register's first report, which is the
  105: 	    "1 of 4 kinds reported" hazard #435's own comment records.  */
  106: 	int		intmask_lowbits_reported[MAX_CPUS];
  107: 
  108: 	/*  Timer stuff  */
  109: 	struct timer	*timer;
  110: 	int		pending_timer_interrupts;
  111: 	struct interrupt timer_irq;
  112: 
  113: 	bool		using_framebuffer;
  114: 
  115: 	/*  LCD at PIO port 1:  */
  116: 	uint8_t		lcd[40 * 2];
  117: 	uint8_t		pio1c;
  118: 	int		lcd_x;
  119: 	int		lcd_y;
  120: 
  121: 	/*  sio: Serial controller, two channels.  */
  122: 	int		console_handle;
  123: 	struct interrupt sio_irq;
  124: 	int		sio_tx_pending[2];
  125: 	int		obio_sio_regno[2];
  126: 	uint8_t		obio_sio_rr[2][8];
  127: 	uint8_t		obio_sio_wr[2][8];
  128: 	uint8_t		sio_queue[2][SIO_QUEUE_SIZE];
  129: 	int		sio_queue_head[2];
  130: 	int		sio_queue_tail[2];
  131: 	int		mouse_enable;
  132: 	int		mouse_buttons;
  133: 
  134: 	/*  ROM and RAM (used by the Ethernet interface)  */
  135: 	uint32_t	fuse_rom[FUSE_ROM_SPACE / sizeof(uint32_t)];
  136: 	uint8_t		nvram[NVRAM_SPACE];
  137: 	uint8_t		tri_port_ram[TRI_PORT_RAM_SPACE];
  138: };
  139: 
  140: 
  141: static int anything_in_sio_queue(struct luna88k_data* d, int n)
  142: {
  143: 	return d->sio_queue_head[n] != d->sio_queue_tail[n];
  144: }
  145: 
  146: static int space_available_in_sio_queue(struct luna88k_data* d, int n)
  147: {
  148: 	int diff = d->sio_queue_head[n] - d->sio_queue_tail[n];
  149: 	if (diff <= 0)
  150: 		diff += SIO_QUEUE_SIZE;
  151: 
  152: 	return diff;
  153: }
  154: 
  155: static uint8_t get_from_sio_queue(struct luna88k_data* d, int n)
  156: {
  157: 	uint8_t result = d->sio_queue[n][d->sio_queue_head[n]++];
  158: 	if (d->sio_queue_head[n] >= SIO_QUEUE_SIZE)
  159: 		d->sio_queue_head[n] = 0;
  160: 
  161: 	return result;
  162: }
  163: 
  164: static void add_to_sio_queue(struct luna88k_data* d, int n, uint8_t c)
  165: {
  166: 	d->sio_queue[n][d->sio_queue_tail[n]++] = c;
  167: 	if (d->sio_queue_tail[n] >= SIO_QUEUE_SIZE)
  168: 		d->sio_queue_tail[n] = 0;
  169: 
  170: 	if (d->sio_queue_tail[n] == d->sio_queue_head[n])
  171: 		fatal("[ luna88k: add_to_sio_queue overrun; increase SIO_QUEUE_SIZE ]\n");
  172: }
  173: 
  174: static void reassert_interrupts(struct luna88k_data *d)
  175: {
  176: 	int cpu;
  177: 
  178: 	for (cpu = 0; cpu < d->ncpus; cpu++) {		/*  #503  */
  179: 		// printf("cpu%i: status = 0x%08x, enable = 0x%08x\n",
  180: 		//	cpu, d->interrupt_status, d->interrupt_enable[0]);
  181: 
  182: 		// General interrupt status is for the entire machine,
  183: 		// but the Software interrupts (IPIs) are per CPU.
  184: 		uint32_t status = d->interrupt_status;
  185: 		uint32_t enable = d->interrupt_enable[cpu];
  186: 
  187: 		if (d->software_interrupt_status[cpu])
  188: 			status |= (1 << 26);
  189: 
  190: 	        if (status & enable) {
  191: 			if (!d->irqActive[cpu])
  192: 		                INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->cpu_irq[cpu]);
  193: 
  194: 			d->irqActive[cpu] = 1;
  195: 		} else {
  196: 			if (d->irqActive[cpu])
  197: 		                INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->cpu_irq[cpu]);
  198: 
  199: 			d->irqActive[cpu] = 0;
  200: 		}
  201: 	}
  202: 
  203: 	// printf("--\n");
  204: }
  205: 
  206: static void luna88k_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
  207: {
  208:         struct luna88k_data *d = (struct luna88k_data *) interrupt->extra;
  209: 	d->interrupt_status |= (1 << (interrupt->line + 25));
  210: 	reassert_interrupts(d);
  211: }
  212: 
  213: static void luna88k_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
  214: {
  215:         struct luna88k_data *d = (struct luna88k_data *) interrupt->extra;
  216: 	d->interrupt_status &= ~(1 << (interrupt->line + 25));
  217: 	reassert_interrupts(d);
  218: }
  219: 
  220: static void reassert_timer_interrupt(struct luna88k_data* d)

   28:  *  COMMENT: LUNA88K machine
   29:  *
   30:  *  This is for experiments with OpenBSD/luna88k. See
   31:  *  openbsd/sys/arch/luna88k/luna88k/locore0.S for more information about
   32:  *  how OpenBSD starts up on this platform.
   33:  */
   34: 
   35: #include <stdio.h>
   36: #include <stdlib.h>
   37: #include <string.h>
   38: 
   39: #include "cpu.h"
   40: #include "device.h"
   41: #include "devices.h"
   42: #include "machine.h"
   43: #include "memory.h"
   44: #include "misc.h"
   45: 
   46: #include "thirdparty/luna88k_board.h"
   47: 
   48: 
   49: MACHINE_SETUP(luna88k)
   50: {
   51: 	const char* luna88k2_fuse_string = "MNAME=LUNA88K+";
   52: 	size_t i;
   53: 
   54: 	device_add(machine, "luna88k");
   55: 
   56: 	if (machine->physical_ram_in_mb > MAXPHYSMEM / 1048576)
   57: 		debugmsg(SUBSYS_MACHINE, "memory", VERBOSITY_WARNING,
   58: 		    "WARNING: %i MB RAM is more than MAXPHYSMEM (%i MB)!",
   59: 		    machine->physical_ram_in_mb,
   60: 		    MAXPHYSMEM / 1048576);
   61: 
   62: 	switch (machine->machine_subtype) {
   63: 
   64: 	case MACHINE_LUNA_88K:
   65: 		machine->machine_name = strdup("LUNA 88K");
   66: 		if (machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 64)
   67: 			debugmsg(SUBSYS_MACHINE, "memory", VERBOSITY_WARNING,
   68: 			    "WARNING: %i MB RAM is more than 64 MB (max on a real LUNA 88K)!",
   69: 			    machine->physical_ram_in_mb);
   70: 		break;
   71: 
   72: 	case MACHINE_LUNA_88K2:
   73: 		machine->machine_name = strdup("LUNA 88K2");
   74: 		if (machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 112)
   75: 			debugmsg(SUBSYS_MACHINE, "memory", VERBOSITY_WARNING,
   76: 			    "WARNING: %i MB RAM is more than 112 MB (max on a real LUNA 88K2)!",
   77: 			    machine->physical_ram_in_mb);
   78: 
   79: 		/*  According to OpenBSD source code,
   80: 		    the string "MNAME=LUNA88K+" in FUSE_ROM_DATA
   81: 		    is used to determine that this is a 88K2, and
   82: 		    not an 88K.
   83: 	                fuse_rom_data[i] =
   84: 	                    (char)((((p->h) >> 24) & 0x000000f0) |
   85: 	                           (((p->l) >> 28) & 0x0000000f));
   86: 		    where h is first 32-bit word, l is second.
   87: 		*/
   88: 
   89: 		for (i = 0; i < strlen(luna88k2_fuse_string); ++i) {
   90: 			uint32_t h = luna88k2_fuse_string[i] & 0xf0;
   91: 			uint32_t l = luna88k2_fuse_string[i] & 0x0f;
   92: 			store_32bit_word(cpu, FUSE_ROM_ADDR + i * 8 + 0, h << 24);
   93: 			store_32bit_word(cpu, FUSE_ROM_ADDR + i * 8 + 4, l << 28);
   94: 		}
   95: 
   96: 		break;
   97: 
   98: 	default:fatal("Unimplemented LUNA88K machine subtype %i\n",
   99: 		    machine->machine_subtype);
  100: 		exit(1);
  101: 	}
  102: 
  103: 	if (machine->ncpus > 4) {
  104: 		fatal("More than 4 CPUs is not supported for LUNA 88K.\n");
  105: 		exit(1);
  106: 	}
  107: 
  108: 	// Unpause all CPUs. (Normally, the emulator only starts with
  109: 	// one CPU running, and it is up to the OS to enable the rest,
  110: 	// but on real LUNA-88K machines, all CPUs are started to run
  111: 	// the OS kernel code simultaneously and it is up to the OS
  112: 	// kernel's initial code to sort things out.)
  113: 	for (i = 0; i < (size_t)machine->ncpus; ++i)
  114: 		machine->cpus[i]->running = true;
  115: 
  116: 	// machine_add_devices_as_symbols(machine, 0);
  117: 
  118: 	if (!machine->prom_emulation)
  119: 		return;
  120: 
  121: 	luna88kprom_init(machine);
  122: }
  123: 
  124: 
  125: MACHINE_DEFAULT_CPU(luna88k)
  126: {
  127: 	machine->cpu_name = strdup("88100");
  128: }
  129: 
  130: 
  131: MACHINE_DEFAULT_RAM(luna88k)
  132: {
  133: 	// Two OpenBSD dmesgs found on the Internet for a LUNA-88K2 showed
  134: 	// 112 MB of real mem. The max for LUNA-88K was probably 64 MB.
  135: 	if (machine->machine_subtype == MACHINE_LUNA_88K2)
  136: 		machine->physical_ram_in_mb = 112;
  137: 	else
  138: 		machine->physical_ram_in_mb = 64;
  139: }
  140: 
  141:

  143: {
  144: 	MR_DEFAULT(luna88k, "LUNA88K", MACHINE_LUNA88K);
  145: 
  146: 	machine_entry_add_alias(me, "luna88k");
  147:

  149: 	    "luna-88k", NULL);

      Pass 9 (grok) found pass 8's "class closure" had closed the SERIAL-MODE bit at ONE
      arm of seven while the contract claimed the axis, and corrected the denominator:
      SIX settable bits, so 7 arms x 6 = 42 less 2 MODE-unreachable = 40, not 35.  v17
      closes all seven.  Table 14 measured all three predicted sweeps at 54/54 on the
      frozen v16 instrument, each killed on v17 by its own row.
    
      Pass 10 (kimi) found TWO WRONG NUMBERS IN MY PACKET.  The control's frozen-v16
      score read 23/41, which is its score on the v10 instrument -- 41 could not be a
      v16 denominator at all, so the DENOMINATOR was the tell for the third table
      running and I did not check it.  And "the previously-closed 23" was sourced from
      nothing; kimi derived 34 of 40 and a deriver written since confirms it exactly.
      That unsourced 23 had already done damage: I used it an hour earlier to refute
      minimax's arithmetic, so that refutation was wrong.  regress/artifacts/

      the GRID rather than the count, because a per-cell breakdown is checkable.
    
      Pass 11 OVERTURNED MY OWN FILING.  I filed the re-route's trap side x {MODE,SFD1}
      as clause-(e) debt; grok ruled FLOOR on a precedent in this round's own records
      (PRETABLE12 graded the same inner `if' L2 for IND/C), and KIMI'S VERDICT LINE

    1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
    2: """m88kexcdeliver -- rung-3 witness AND detector: does the m88k exception core DELIVER a trap-like
    3: exception (vector entry reached, shadow registers right, machine still running), or stop the machine?
    4: 
    5: `cpu_m88k.c`'s exception switch marks only RESET / INTERRUPT / INSTRUCTION_ACCESS / DATA_ACCESS
    6: `implemented`; every other vector below M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START falls to a fallback that
    7: prints `unimplemented vector=0x%03x` and sets `running = false` on EVERY guest CPU.  The vector-
    8: specific tail switch then has a `default:` that stops THIS cpu with `internal error` -- so a fix
    9: needs BOTH `implemented = true` AND a tail arm (the naive one-site fix was MEASURED to halt, pass 1).
   10: Real hardware delivers VBR + 8*vector (MC88100 UM Table 6-1, mc88100.txt:8524-8538) and the guest OS
   11: turns it into a signal; rte resumes at SNIP (:8983-8985), the destination register is unchanged
   12: (6.6 :9305-9308; 6.8.3 :10300-10305).
   13: 
   14: Cold-debugger probe on the COMMITTED `testm88k` machine (no machine or device edits).  Per row: a
   15: guest program at 0x10000 planted with `put w` (liveness marker `addu r9,r0,r10` first), registers
   16: seeded with `rN=`, a `br .` (c0000000) planted at every vector entry so a delivered exception spins
   17: rather than executing zeros, `breakpoint add <8*vector>`, `continue`, then `reg` and `reg 0,0`.
   18: 
   19:   row        program (after the marker at 0x10000)                 raise                     entry
   20:   div0       divu r2,r3,r0            f4436800                    ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE 8  0x40
   21:   ovf        add  r2,r3,r4 (7fffffff+1) f4437004                  INTEGER_OVERFLOW 9        0x48
   22:   misalign   ld   r2,r3,0 (r3 odd)    14430000 (major opcode 5)   MISALIGNED_ACCESS 4       0x20
   23:   bounds     tb1  0,r3,7 (r3=1)       f003d807  supervisor trap   BOUNDS_CHECK 7            0x38
   24:   priv       stcr r3,cr1 (r3=0x2: MODE clear) 80038023; ldcr r2,cr1 80404020   PRIVILEGE 6  0x30
   25:   privtb     stcr r3,cr1; tb1 0,r4,8 (f004d808) in user mode      PRIVILEGE 6               0x30
   26:   unimpl     stcr r3,cr1; prom_call f400fc92 in user mode         UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE 5    0x28
   27:   error      div0 whose handler at 0x40 is a misaligned ld: the 2nd fault with SFRZ set re-routes
   28:              to ERROR 10                                                                    0x50
   29:   dslot      br.n +16 (c4000004) with divu r2,r3,r0 in its delay slot -> the else-arm of the
   30:              delay-slot branch (SNIP = pc+4 = 0x1000c, SFIP = delay_target = 0x10014 -- two
   31:              DIFFERENT numbers; with br.n +8 both were 0x1000e and the SFIP channel was vacuous)  ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE 0x40
   32:              *** THIS ROW PINS THE ELSE-ARM AS IMPLEMENTED, NOT AS THE MANUAL'S CONTRACT.  Table 6-3 (the shadows copy
   33:              XIP/NIP/FIP) with the pointer definitions at UM 6.2 (mc88100.txt:2093-2104: FIP is the taken branch's
   34:              target while the branch executes, so for a fault in its delay slot NIP = target, FIP = target+4) and X(rte)'s
   35:              `fip != nip + 4' arm say the else-arm returns WRONG (codex, grok, pass 2: the fallthrough executes
   36:              before the target).  Pre-existing, out of this diff's scope, filed as m88kdslotshadow; when that round
   37:              lands this row is RE-PINNED FIRST (oracle-correction rule: re-pin, measure HEAD red, then fix). ***
   38:   errortb    tb1  0,r3,10 (r3=1)      f003d80a  supervisor trap naming ERROR (10) directly -- the
   39:              only route to switch 1's ERROR arm (the double-fault route enters at the re-route AFTER
   40:              switch 1, so it cannot see that arm: M_ERRORDROP escaped the error row 9/9, table 1)  0x50
   41:   misaligntb/unimpltb/div0tb/ovftb   tb1 0,r3,{4,5,8,9} (f003d804/05/08/09), supervisor: the TRAP
   42:              ROUTE into switch 1 for the four classes whose other rows arrive by instruction --
   43:              `if (!is_trap) implemented = true' escapes those rows (grok, deepseek, pass 2)  0x20/0x28/0x40/0x48
   44:   vbrtb      stcr r5,cr7 (800580e5, r5=0x2000) then tb1 0,r3,7: the vector table RELOCATED -> entry
   45:              0x2038, not 0x38.  Every other row runs with VBR == 0, so `pc = 8*vector' (VBR dropped)
   46:              was a 10/10 escape (Opus, pass 2).  The one ANCHORED row: it alone reds if the table moves.  0x2038
   47:   trapfrozen div0 whose handler at 0x40 is tb1 0,r3,7 (r3=0x11): a TRAP with SFRZ already set is NOT
   48:              re-routed to ERROR (UM :8633-8636, 6.10 :10613-10614) -- it delivers to 0x38 with the
   49:              shadows still frozen.  `if (!is_trap)' -> `if (true)' was a 10/10 escape (Opus, pass 2)  0x38
   50:   trapfrozen4/5/6/9   the same shape naming 4, 5, 6, 9: the {SFRZ set, is_trap} x {class} closure -- with only class 7 rowed,
   51:              `if (!is_trap || vector == <class>)' re-routed that one frozen trap to ERROR unseen (codex, pass 5)  0x20/0x28/0x30/0x48
   52:   trapfrozen8 first fault add 7fffffff+1 (handler planted at 0x48), handler tb1 0,r3,8: the frozen trap naming DIV0
   53:              delivers to 0x40 -- observable only because the handler is NOT at 0x40 for this row.                     0x40
   54:   trapfrozen10 the same shape naming 10: for the RE-ROUTE mutant it is equivalent (0x50 either way), but switch 1's ERROR
   55:              arm is a separate predicate -- a frozen trap naming 10 enters switch 1 AS ERROR, which no other row does
   56:              (the err* rows enter under their own classes and become ERROR after the fallback); `implemented =
   57:              !(PSR & SFRZ)' there sends it to the fallback -- M_ERRORFRZOFF (codex, pass 6).  Expect 0x50, shadows frozen 0x50
   58:   privsuptb  tb1 0,r3,6 (f003d806) in SUPERVISOR mode: the trap route into PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION.  privtb is
   59:              NOT that route -- the user-mode filter in X(tb0) raises PRIVILEGE with is_trap == 0, so
   60:              `implemented = !is_trap' on the PRIVILEGE arm left priv AND privtb green (codex, glm: pass 2)  0x30
   61:   usertb     tb1 0,r3,128 (f003d880): the first USER trap vector, delivered by the pre-existing code to
   62:              0x400.  Pins the tail switch's `< USER_TRAPS_START' guard: `<=' sends 128 into the tail
   63:              default and stops the cpu (codex's one-character escape, pass 2)                       0x400
   64:   resid50    tb1 0,r3,50 (f003d832): a residual vector.  EXPECTED RESULT = HALT (the fallback, by design).
   65:   resid127   tb1 0,r3,127 (f003d87f): the LAST residual vector -- pins the fallback window's FAR bound (`vector < 128').
   66:              The interior pins were resid50 (50) and sfu1h (114: its nested SFU1 halts at the fallback), so a shrink of the
   67:              bound to any N in (114, 128) escaped BY CONSTRUCTION (measured: `< 127' 29/29 on v5, `< 51' 28/29 -- sfu1h saw
   68:              that one), and usertb (128, just outside) pins only that 128 is not halted: the accept-row-at-the-LAST-slot
   69:              rule (deepseek and glm, pass 5, from the packet alone; glm's [115,128] named it -- the shrink VALUES are
   70:              115..127, 128 being the bound itself: deepseek, pass 6).  EXPECTED RESULT = HALT
   71:              naming 0x07f; a shrunk bound delivers 127 to 0x3f8
   72:              and the tail default stops the cpu instead (TAILHALT) -- mutants M_FALLBACK127, M_FALLBACK51.
   73:   resid11    tb1 0,r3,11 (f003d80b): the FIRST residual vector (M88K_EXCEPTION_NMI in cpu_m88k.h, unimplemented in switch 1 --
   74:              grok, pass 7) -- the fallback window's NEAR bound.  `vector >= 12 &&' on the
   75:              fallback swept 36/36 because no row named 11..49 (kimi, pass 6).  EXPECTED RESULT = HALT naming 0x00b        (0x58)
   76:   resid50ind stcr r5,cr1 (r5=0x80000000: IND CLEAR) then tb1 0,r3,50: the fallback with IND clear -- `&& (PSR & IND)' on the
   77:              fallback swept 36/36 because every fallback row had IND set.  EXPECTED RESULT = HALT naming 0x032          (0x190)
   78:   resid50c   stcr r5,cr1 (r5=0x90000002: C SET) then tb1 0,r3,50: the fallback with C set -- `&& !(PSR & C)' swept likewise.
   79:              With usfu1 (MODE clear, SFD1 set), sfu1h (SFRZ set) and resid50ser (SER set) these give every settable PSR
   80:              bit both sides at the fallback -- SIX bits, not five.  *** THIS SENTENCE WAS FALSE FROM v16 UNTIL v17 and
   81:              said `every settable bit' the whole time: it was written when SER was believed 88110-only, and when the
   82:              MC88100 manual settled that it is software-settable the claim silently became an overclaim with no row
   83:              behind it.  minimax asked on pass 10 whether the blurb matched its rows; it did not, and resid50ser is what
   84:              makes it true.  A claim that enumerates its evidence stays checkable when the world changes underneath it;
   85:              one that says `every' does not. ***  MXM and BO cannot be set by stcr without stopping the cpu -- disclosed.
   86:              EXPECTED RESULT = HALT    (0x190)
   87:   trapfrozen450 / trapfrozen496  the same shape naming 0x1c2 (the OpenBSD >= 5.3 syscall vector) and MVMEPROM_VECTOR: the LAST
   88:              two switch-1 labels.  Switch 1 has TEN deliverable labels -- 4..10, 128, 450, 496 -- and the frozen-trap rows now
   89:              cover all ten; `!is_trap || vector == 0x1c2' swept 41/41 before these (codex, pass 7)                  0xe10 / 0xf80
   90:   trapfrozen511 the same shape naming 511, the LAST nameable trap vector (tb1's field is 9 bits): trapfrozen128 pinned only the
   91:              near edge of the user range -- `!is_trap || vector >= 129' swept (kimi, pass 7; the last-slot rule again)          0xff8
   92:   trapfrozen128 div0 whose handler is tb1 0,r3,128: a frozen USER trap is exempted from the re-route like any trap and
   93:              delivered to 0x400 with frozen shadows -- `!is_trap || vector >= 128' swept 36/36 (kimi, pass 6)              0x400
   94:   usfu1      stcr r5,cr1 (r5=0x0000000a: USER mode, SFD1 set) then a LEGAL divu r6,r5,r4: SFU1 (114) raised from USER
   95:              mode reaches the fallback -- the only user-mode route to it (a user tb1 naming < 128 raises PRIVILEGE).
   96:              Every other fallback row is supervisor, so `&& (PSR & MODE)' on the fallback predicate swept 29/29 (kimi,
   97:              pass 5, MEASURED).  EXPECTED RESULT = HALT naming 0x072; the mutant delivers to 0x390 and the #if 0'd SFU1
   98:              tail arm makes that TAILHALT -- mutant M_FBSUPONLY                                                (0x390)
   99:              Negative control: the shipped form must NOT deliver it (rejected D4 would)             (0x190)
  100:   sfu1h      div0 whose handler at 0x40 is a LEGAL divu r6,r5,r4: with SFD1 set the divide raises
  101:              SFU1_PRECISE (114), which halts at the FALLBACK (it runs before the SFRZ re-route).  EXPECTED
  102:              RESULT = HALT naming vector 0x072, with pc == 0x40 (the first delivery happened -- on HEAD the
  103:              first divu halts on vector 0x008, not this row's 0x072, and it reads STOP).  With the fallback off (M_DELIVERALL) the
  104:              nested SFU1 is RE-ROUTED TO ERROR (0x50), not delivered to 0x390: the row reds as STOP.  So it
  105:              pins 'SFU1 inside a handler still halts', NOT 'SFU1 is not delivered to its vector' -- the
  106:              first-exception SFU1 route is row m88kexcsfu1's (grok, kimi, pass 3)                    (0x390)
  107:   udiv0      stcr r3,cr1 (r3=0x2: user mode) then divu r2,r3,r0: the fallback comment's own sentence,
  108:              'one user-mode divu by zero ended the emulation', measured literally; also pins that user mode
  109:              with SFD1 CLEAR yields vector 8, not SFU1 (the m88kexcsfu1 boundary)              ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE 0x40
  110:   indclr     stcr r5,cr1 (r5=0x80000000: MODE only, IND CLEAR) then add 7fffffff+1: the exception path must
  111:              SET IND (PSR 0x8000000b from 0x80000000; EPSR 0x80000000).  Every other row's pre-fault PSR already
  112:              has IND, so `| M88K_PSR_IND' was unobservable -- the carry shape again (grok, codex, pass 3)     0x48
  113:   umisalign  stcr r5,cr1 (r5=0x2: user mode) then ld r2,r3,0 with r3 odd: MISALIGNED from USER mode
  114:              (a MODE-conditioned `implemented' on that arm swept 23/23 -- Opus, pass 4)                    0x20
  115:   uovf       stcr r5,cr1 (user mode) then add 7fffffff+1: OVERFLOW from USER mode -- the measured
  116:              M_OVFSUPONLY escape (a user-mode overflow ended the emulation again, 23/23)               0x48
  117:   errovf     div0 whose handler at 0x40 is add 7fffffff+1: a NON-TRAP fault of a SECOND class with
  118:              SFRZ set -> re-routed to ERROR with frozen shadows (M_SFRZMIS -- `!is_trap && vector ==
  119:              MISALIGNED' on the re-route -- swept 23/23 because `error' was that cell's only row)      0x50
  120:   errind     div0 whose handler is stcr r4,cr1 (r4=0x80000009: SFRZ kept, IND CLEAR) then the odd ld: the re-route with
  121:              interrupts enabled -- every SFRZ-set row had reached it with IND set, so `(PSR & SFRZ) && (PSR & IND)'
  122:              swept (grok, pass 7).  Expect ERROR at 0x50, frozen shadows, the diagnostic present                     0x50
  123:   errc       the same with r4=0x9000000b (C SET): `&& !(PSR & C)' on the re-route swept likewise; expects PSR 0x9000000b after
  124:              delivery (C survives exception entry -- the row first shipped wanting 0x8000000b and BASE caught it).  MODE (errpriv) and SFD1
  125:              (errdiv0) already vary at the re-route; MXM/BO cannot be set by stcr without stopping the cpu; SER is varied by trapfrozenser 0x50
  126:   errdiv0    div0 whose handler is stcr r5,cr1 (r5=0x80000003: SFD1 CLEAR, SFRZ kept) then divu by zero:
  127:              a frozen non-trap DIV0 -> ERROR (with SFD1 set the same divu raises SFU1 instead: sfu1h)     0x50
  128:   errpriv    div0 whose handler is stcr r5,cr1 (r5=0x00000003: USER mode, SFRZ kept) then ldcr: a frozen
  129:              non-trap PRIVILEGE -> ERROR (Fable, pass 4: the {SFRZ, !is_trap} x {class} closure)        0x50
  130:   errunimpl  div0 whose handler is stcr r5,cr1 (user mode, SFRZ kept) then prom_call: a frozen non-trap
  131:              UNIMPLEMENTED -> ERROR.  BOUNDS and ERROR have no non-trap route (trapfrozen covers the trap)  0x50
  132:   ovfc       stcr r5,cr1 (80058025, r5=0x90000002: C set) then add 7fffffff+1: the carry bit must
  133:              SURVIVE the exception path (UM 6.6) -> PSR 0x9000000b, EPSR 0x90000002.  Every other
  134:              row's pre-fault C is 0, so PSR == 0x8000000b pinned 'C not set', never 'C unchanged'  0x48
  135:   live       divu r2,r3,r4  (r3=0x60, r4=0x10) -> r2 = 6           none      LIVENESS control
  136:   attrib     divu r2,r3,r4  (r3=0x10, r4=0x10) -> r2 = 1           none      ATTRIBUTION control
  137:   selftest   no program: `reg 0,0` at reset must parse PSR == 0x80000002 (the CR parse works)
  138: 
  139: RESULT per fault row (the classifier tests the STOP channel before pc, deliberately: the naive fix
  140: sets pc = entry and THEN stops, so pc alone cannot tell it from delivery):
  141:   HALT       `unimplemented vector=` printed AND the machine stopped (both observed, pass 3); an
  142:              expected-HALT row (resid50, resid127, sfu1h, usfu1) must also see ITS vector named in the diagnostic
  143:   TAILHALT   `internal error` printed and the machine stopped (the naive fix's shape)
  144:   STOP       the machine stopped for another reason (e.g. a wrong vector base landed in zeros)
  145:   BADREGS    the entry breakpoint fired but a shadow-register/PSR/destination assert failed, or (bad=refatal) the re-route's
  146:              `[ SFRZ already set in PSR => ERROR ]' line was present on a frozen-TRAP row or absent on a re-routed err* row --
  147:              the channel that separates the exemption arm from the re-route arm when both deliver to 0x50 (kimi, pass 6), or
  148:              (bad=level) the transcript carried an `exception <CLASS>' line: the class printed at ERROR
  149:              level, which the fork's default WARNING verbosity shows and INFO does not (kills M_LEVEL)
  150:   (rows resid50, resid127, sfu1h and usfu1 EXPECT HALT: the fallback stopping the machine is the correct answer there)
  151:   DELIVER    entry breakpoint fired, machine running, and every assert holds:
  152:              pc == entry, SXIP == fault_pc|V, SNIP, SFIP, PSR == 0x8000000b (SFRZ|IND|SFD1|MODE),
  153:              EPSR == the pre-fault PSR (0x80000002 supervisor rows; 0x00000002 user-mode rows),
  154:              r2 unchanged (0xdeadbeef)
  155:   NOSTART    the liveness marker never ran
  156: Controls print DONE (r2 == the expected quotient, machine running) or a failure word.
  157: 
  158: Output: one `M88KEXC_ROW=<name> RESULT=<word> ...` line per row, `M88KEXC_RESULT=<delivered>/<fault rows>`,
  159: `M88KEXC_SELFTEST=OK|FAIL`, `M88KEXC_DONE`.  Set M88KEXC_VERBOSE=1 for transcripts.
  160: 
  161: CONTRACT (clause e): catches a class returned to the fallback (HALT) by either route (instruction rows
  162: and the *tb trap rows); a SHRUNK fallback window (resid127 pins the far bound: `< 128' -> `< 127' or `< 51' both deliver 127 to the
  163: tail default, TAILHALT; usertb pins that 128 is outside it, resid50 the interior); a class reaching the tail default (TAILHALT); a wrong vector SCALING (M_VEC4,
  164: M_VEC7: STOP) and a DROPPED vector base (vbrtb: STOP); a stale SXIP/SNIP/SFIP; a wrong PSR, including a
  165: cleared carry (ovfc); a dropped IND (indclr); the debugmsg LEVEL regression to ERROR/WARNING (bad=level on any
  166: delivered row: those print at the post-startup WARNING floor, INFO and DEBUG do not -- so exact INFO is NOT pinned,
  167: only 'not ERROR/WARNING' -- M_LEVEL; codex, pass 3); a skipped EPSR save; a skipped supervisor switch (the user-mode rows: EPSR MODE
  168: clear vs PSR MODE set); a MODE-conditioned `implemented' on any class that user mode can raise ({class} x
  169: {MODE} closure: DIV0 udiv0, PRIVILEGE priv/privtb, UNIMPLEMENTED unimpl, MISALIGNED umisalign, OVERFLOW uovf;
  170: BOUNDS and ERROR have no user-mode route -- a user tb1 naming them raises PRIVILEGE, and every handler runs
  171: supervisor; the FALLBACK predicate itself is pinned in user mode by usfu1, the one user-mode route to it); a class-conditioned SFRZ re-route, its diagnostic pinned both ways by the refatal channel, and a PSR-bit conjunct on the re-route (the
  172: predicate reads PSR: every stcr-settable 88100 bit now varies there -- MODE errpriv, SFD1 errdiv0, IND errind, C errc; MXM/BO cannot be
  173: set by stcr without stopping the cpu, and SER is NOT an 88110-only bit -- mc88100.txt, anchor `SER - Serial Mode', documents it
  174: for the MC88100 as software-settable and m88k_stcr stores it verbatim, so trapfrozenser rows it.  *** AND THE CONFIGURATION CLAIM
  175: THIS DOCSTRING USED TO MAKE WAS FALSE TWICE OVER: it said the 88110-only bits `are 88100 here', and this probe launches
  176: `-E testm88k' with NO `-C', while machine_test.c's MACHINE_DEFAULT_CPU(testm88k) sets cpu_name to "88110".  Every table this
  177: round has run measured an 88110.  The SCORES stand -- nothing in the m88k model reads cpu_type to change behaviour outside the
  178: name and the PID register, so the exception path is shared -- but the CLAIM did not, and pinning `-C 88100' with a PID control
  179: row is owed as its own revision with its own BASE run (codex, pass 8). ***) ({SFRZ set, !is_trap} x {class}: MISALIGNED error, OVERFLOW errovf,
  180: DIV0 errdiv0 -- the handler clears SFD1 first, with it set the divu raises SFU1 instead (sfu1h) --, PRIVILEGE errpriv
  181: and UNIMPLEMENTED errunimpl -- the handler drops to user mode keeping SFRZ; BOUNDS and ERROR have no non-trap route,
  182: the frozen TRAP is trapfrozen); a wrong return address; the delay-slot else-arm's SFIP source (dslot, +16
  183: geometry); a trap re-routed to ERROR while frozen, per class ({SFRZ set, is_trap} x {every switch-1 label a trap can NAME -- 0..3 are deliverable too and are unrowed, disclosed as
  184: m88kexchwtraps; the word EVERY overclaimed until v16}: trapfrozen (7),
  185: trapfrozen4/5/6/8/9/10/128/450/496/511 -- the ten labels 4..10, 128, 0x1c2, MVMEPROM_VECTOR, enumerated from switch 1's `case' list, plus
  186: the far edge of the user range (511) --
  187: class 10's re-route exemption is told from the re-route by the refatal channel (the pass-6 packet called it equivalent; kimi refuted
  188: that: the re-route prints a diagnostic the exemption does not) and switch 1's ERROR eligibility under SFRZ by trapfrozen10's
  189: HALT/DELIVER; 0..3 are the hardware-event tails, m88kexchwtraps); the fallback window's NEAR bound (resid11) and the fallback predicate
  190: conditioned on any settable PSR bit (resid50ind, resid50c, resid50ser, usfu1, sfu1h -- SIX bits, and resid50ser is
  191: what made that claim true when SER turned out to be settable); a written destination.  Does NOT catch:
  192: fcr2/fcr4/fcr5; scoreboard (SSBR) bits; DMT contents; the post-delivery
  193: `cpu->delay_slot' write on the NON-delay path (M_DELAYALWAYS: needs a second UNFROZEN exception -- filed);
  194: vectors 0..3's tail arms (no row: M_TAILOFF -- filed); vectors 11..113 and 116..127 (fallback halts by
  195: design); SFU1 (114/115, row m88kexcsfu1); `implemented = is_trap' on BOUNDS/ERROR (EQUIVALENT today: no
  196: instruction raises 7 or 10 -- becomes live when tbnd lands, row m88ktbnd); a PSR-bit conjunct on a switch-1 `implemented' arm
  197: OTHER than MODE (closed for every class) and SFRZ at ERROR (both polarities: errortb unfrozen, trapfrozen10 frozen) -- the arms read no PSR
  198: bit, so {arm} x {C, IND, SFD1} is clause-(e) debt, not floor; the representative (MISALIGNED with `!(PSR & C)', M_MISNOC) MEASURED
  199: 41/41 on v10 (the escape, as disclosed) and 45/46 on v14 -- KILLED by errc, whose second fault is MISALIGNED with C set (table 11).  So the
  200: {MISALIGNED} x {C} and {MISALIGNED} x {IND} cells are rowed by errc / errind as a side effect of the re-route rows; the disclosure stands
  201: for the remaining arms -- and that sentence was WRONG THREE WAYS until v16, all three measured (opus5, pass 8): it named
  202: OVERFLOW, which ovfc and indclr already close, since both fault at that arm with C set and IND clear respectively; it
  203: claimed {any arm} x {SFD1}, closed at all seven (at DIV0 architecturally -- X(divu) tests SFD1 BEFORE the divisor, so only
  204: the tb1 route reaches that arm with SFD1 set); and it OMITTED BOUNDS and ERROR, which were open.  v16 closes
  205: {UNIMPL, PRIV, BOUNDS, DIV0, ERROR} x {C, IND} with five rows and the re-route's trap side with three.  The DENOMINATOR is 7 arms x SIX settable bits (8 architectural
  206: minus BO and MXM, which the store path refuses) = 42 cells, minus the 2 MODE-unreachable at BOUNDS/ERROR = 40 -- v16 said 35
  207: and five bits, and grok corrected both on pass 9.  Those two ARE unreachable rather than merely unrowed: neither constant is
  208: raised by any instruction, a user tb1 naming a vector below 128 raises PRIVILEGE with is_trap == 0, and the re-route writes
  209: ERROR after switch 1 so it never enters that arm -- a warrant that EXPIRES when X(tbnd) lands (row m88ktbnd).  The re-route's TRAP side is closed on all five bits that can vary there
  210: (MODE trapfrozenuser -- uniquely, per CTL_RRTRAPINV; SFD1 trapfrozensfd1 AND trapfrozenuser, which clears both bits;
  211: IND trapfrozenind, C trapfrozenc, SER trapfrozenser; SFRZ is invariant BY CONSTRUCTION, since it is the guard) -- v17 closed three of those five and left MODE and SFD1, and pass 11 graded that
  212: L2 rather than debt on two precedents from this round's own records.  What remains
  213: disclosed: MULTI-AXIS joints of two or more PSR bits at one arm (clause (e) by the carrier's own bound), and vectors 0..3
  214: (m88kexchwtraps).
  215: """
  216: import os, pty, re, select, struct, sys, time
  217: 
  218: BIN = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "/tmp/gxsec-build/gxemul"
  219: CODE = 0x10000
  220: DATA = 0x20000
  221: XIP_V = 0x2
  222: PSR_RESET = 0x80000002          # MODE | IND (cpu_m88k.c: cr[PSR] = M88K_PSR_MODE | M88K_PSR_IND)
  223: PSR_EXC = 0x8000000b            # MODE | SFD1 | IND | SFRZ after the common path
  224: PSR_USER = 0x00000002           # IND only: what the user-mode rows write via stcr
  225: PSR_FRZSUP = 0x80000003         # MODE | IND | SFRZ, SFD1 CLEAR: a frozen handler that wants its divu to raise DIV0, not SFU1
  226: PSR_FRZUSER = 0x00000003        # IND | SFRZ: a frozen handler dropping to user mode (PRIVILEGE / UNIMPLEMENTED routes).
  227:                                 # *** MODE **AND SFD1** ARE BOTH CLEAR HERE, and this comment said only MODE until v20.
  228:                                 # That omission cost a prediction: PRETABLE15 expected M_RRTRAPSFD1 to red trapfrozensfd1
  229:                                 # alone (58/59) and it measured 57/59, because trapfrozenuser -- which uses this value --
  230:                                 # presents SFD1 clear as well.  A constant's comment that lists SOME of the bits it clears
  231:                                 # reads like a complete decode and is not one.
  232: PSR_USERSFD1 = 0x0000000a       # IND | SFD1, MODE CLEAR: user mode with the FPU disabled -- a legal divu raises SFU1 (kimi, pass 5)
  233: PSR_FRZNOIND = 0x80000009       # MODE | SFD1 | SFRZ, IND CLEAR: a frozen handler that re-enables interrupts (grok, pass 7)
  234: PSR_FRZC = 0x9000000b           # C | MODE | SFD1 | IND | SFRZ: a frozen handler with the carry bit set (grok, pass 7)
  235: PSR_SUPCNOIND = 0xb0000000      # SER | MODE | C, IND CLEAR: supervisor, carry set, interrupts enabled, SERIAL MODE set.  ONE value
  236:                                 # closes the C, IND and SER cells at an arm together (grok, pass 9: v16 set only MODE|C here and closed
  237:                                 # SER at ONE arm, which is the cell-not-class error inside the repair for that error).  ONE value closes BOTH
  238:                                 # open cells at a switch-1 arm, because the open cells are always (C never set) and (IND never clear)
  239:                                 # -- opus5's census, pass 8.  The same trick ovfc and indclr already play at the OVERFLOW arm.
  240: PSR_FRZSER = 0xa000000b         # SER | MODE | SFD1 | IND | SFRZ: a frozen handler with SERIAL MODE set.  SER is NOT an 88110-only bit:
  241: PSR_SERSUP = 0xa0000002         # SER | MODE | IND: supervisor with serial mode set and nothing else unusual -- the pre-fault value for
  242:                                 # the fallback and non-trap rows that close SER at those two sites (grok, pass 9)
  243:                                 # mc88100.txt, anchor `SER - Serial Mode', says "This bit is set by software as appropriate", and
  244:                                 # m88k_stcr stores PSR verbatim after its BO / MXM / MODE checks.  The fork's vendored header comments
  245:                                 # it `/* 88110 serial mode */', which is upstream's error and which this probe's contract had copied
  246:                                 # (codex, pass 8; checked against the manual on disk rather than against the header).
  247: 
  248: def tri(sub, d, s1, s2):
  249:     return 0xf4000000 | (d << 21) | (s1 << 16) | (sub << 8) | s2
  250: 
  251: MARK = tri(0x60, 9, 0, 10)                       # addu r9,r0,r10  -> r9 = 0x12345678 (liveness)
  252: SELF = 0xc0000000                                # br . (disp 0)
  253: DIVU_R2_R3_R0 = tri(0x68, 2, 3, 0)               # f4436800
  254: DIVU_R2_R3_R4 = tri(0x68, 2, 3, 4)               # f4436804
  255: DIVU_R6_R5_R4 = tri(0x68, 6, 5, 4)               # f4c56804 -- a LEGAL divide inside a handler: SFD1 is set there, so X(divu) raises
  256:                                                  #   SFU1_PRECISE (114) before it looks at the divisor -> the fallback must still HALT
  257: ADD_R2_R3_R4 = tri(0x70, 2, 3, 4)                # f4437004
  258: LD_R2_R3_0 = 0x14000000 | (2 << 21) | (3 << 16)  # 14430000 (major opcode 5, ld)
  259: TB1_0_R3_7 = 0xf003d807                          # tb1 0,r3,7   (computed vector 7, supervisor)
  260: TB1_0_R4_8 = 0xf004d808                          # tb1 0,r4,8
  261: TB1_0_R3_10 = 0xf003d80a
  262: TB1_0_R3_4 = 0xf003d804                          # tb1 0,r3,4   -- a supervisor trap naming MISALIGNED_ACCESS (4) directly
  263: TB1_0_R3_5 = 0xf003d805                          # tb1 0,r3,5   -- naming UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE (5)
  264: TB1_0_R3_8 = 0xf003d808                          # tb1 0,r3,8   -- naming ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE (8)
  265: TB1_0_R3_9 = 0xf003d809                          # tb1 0,r3,9   -- naming INTEGER_OVERFLOW (9)
  266: TB1_0_R3_6 = 0xf003d806                          # tb1 0,r3,6   -- naming PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION (6): the user-mode tb1 filter raises
  267:                                                  #   PRIVILEGE with is_trap == 0 (cpu_m88k_instr.c X(tb0)), so privtb is NOT the trap route
  268: TB1_0_R3_450 = 0xf003d9c2                        # tb1 0,r3,450 -- the 0x1c2 syscall vector (OpenBSD >= 5.3): a switch-1 label of its own
  269: TB1_0_R3_496 = 0xf003d9f0                        # tb1 0,r3,496 -- MVMEPROM_VECTOR: likewise
  270: TB1_0_R3_511 = 0xf003d9ff                        # tb1 0,r3,511 -- the LAST nameable trap vector (9-bit field): the far edge of the user-trap range
  271: TB1_0_R3_128 = 0xf003d880                        # tb1 0,r3,128 -- the first USER trap vector: delivered by the pre-existing code (entry 0x400)
  272: TB1_0_R3_50 = 0xf003d832                         # tb1 0,r3,50  -- a RESIDUAL vector (11..113): must still HALT (negative control vs D4)
  273: TB1_0_R3_11 = 0xf003d80b                         # tb1 0,r3,11  -- the FIRST residual vector: the fallback window's NEAR bound (kimi, pass 6)
  274: TB1_0_R3_127 = 0xf003d87f                        # tb1 0,r3,127 -- the LAST residual vector: pins the fallback window's FAR bound (< 128),
  275:                                                  #   which resid50 (interior) and usertb (128, outside) cannot see (deepseek, glm, pass 5)
  276: #  The four *tb rows are the TRAP ROUTE into switch 1 for the classes whose other rows arrive by INSTRUCTION
  277: #  (is_trap == 0).  Pass-2 seats (grok, deepseek, independently) predicted `if (!is_trap) implemented = true;'
  278: #  escapes the instruction rows for MISALIGNED/UNIMPL/DIV0/OVF; these rows are the ones that redden it (M_ISTRAP).
  279:                          # tb1 0,r3,10  (a supervisor trap naming vector 10 = ERROR directly)
  280: STCR_R3_CR1 = 0x80038023                         # stcr r3,cr1 (PSR)
  281: STCR_R4_CR1 = 0x80048024                         # stcr r4,cr1 (PSR) -- for handlers whose r5 is the odd load address
  282: STCR_R5_CR1 = 0x80058025                         # stcr r5,cr1 (PSR)  -- same format: 0x80000000 | S1<<16 | 0x8000 | CR<<5 | S1
  283: STCR_R5_CR7 = 0x800580e5                         # stcr r5,cr7 (VBR)
  284: PSR_CARRY = 0x90000002                           # MODE | C | IND: the ovfc row's pre-fault PSR (C = bit 28, UM 6.6 'carry bit unchanged')
  285: PSR_INDCLR = 0x80000000                          # MODE only, IND CLEAR: the indclr row's pre-fault PSR -- every other row starts with IND
  286:                                                  #   already set, so `| M88K_PSR_IND' in the exception-path PSR write was unobservable (grok, codex, pass 3)
  287: VBR_TEST = 0x2000                                # the vbrtb row relocates the vector table here (OpenBSD relocates its own)
  288: LDCR_R2_CR1 = 0x80404020                         # ldcr r2,cr1
  289: PROM_CALL = 0xf400fc92                           # M88K_PROM_INSTR
  290: BR_N_PLUS8 = 0xc4000002
  291: BR_N_PLUS16 = 0xc4000004                         # br.n +16: delay_target (slot+8) != slot_pc+4, so SNIP and SFIP are DIFFERENT numbers                          # br.n +2 words
  292: 
  293: V_MISALIGN, V_UNIMPL, V_PRIV, V_BOUNDS, V_DIV0, V_OVF, V_ERROR = 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10

  485: class Sess:
  486:     def __init__(self):
  487:         stub = "/tmp/gx_m88k_exc_%d.bin" % os.getpid()
  488:         with open(stub, "wb") as f:
  489:             f.write(struct.pack(">I", 0xf4005800))          # or r0,r0,r0 so the machine constructs
  490:         self.pid, self.fd = pty.fork()

  493:             os._exit(127)

  495:     def rd(self, t=0.4):
  496:         r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
  497:         if self.fd not in r:
  498:             return True
  499:         try:
  500:             d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
  501:         except OSError:
  502:             return False
  503:         if not d:
  504:             return False
  505:         self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
  506:         return True
  507:     def wait_prompt(self, mark, timeout=30):
  508:         t = time.time()
  509:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
  510:             if not self.rd():
  511:                 return False
  512:             if len(self.buf) > mark and self.buf[mark:].rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
  513:                 return True
  514:         return False
  515:     def send(self, sx, timeout=30):
  516:         mark = len(self.buf)
  517:         b = (sx + "\n").encode("latin1"); n = 0
  518:         while n < len(b):
  519:             n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
  520:         self.wait_prompt(mark, timeout)
  521:         return mark
  522:     def cr(self, name):
  523:         m = self.send("reg 0,0")
  524:         mm = re.search(r"(?<![A-Z])%s=0x([0-9a-f]{8})" % name, self.buf[m:])
  525:         return int(mm.group(1), 16) if mm else None
  526:     def gpr(self, n):
  527:         m = self.send("reg")
  528:         mm = re.search(r"r%-2d\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})" % n, self.buf[m:])
  529:         return int(mm.group(1), 16) if mm else None
  530:     def pc(self):
  531:         m = self.send("reg")
  532:         mm = re.search(r"pc\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})", self.buf[m:])
  533:         return int(mm.group(1), 16) if mm else None
  534:     def close(self):
  535:         try:
  536:             os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n"); time.sleep(0.3); os.kill(self.pid, 9); os.waitpid(self.pid, 0)
  537:         except Exception:
  538:             pass
  539: 
  540: def fmt(v):
  541:     return ("0x%08x" % v) if isinstance(v, int) else str(v)
  542: 
  543: def run_row(name, prog, regs, vector, fault_pc, epsr_exp, handler, grace=6.0, handler_at=None):
  544:     if handler_at is None:
  545:         handler_at = V_DIV0                              # the first fault of every two-fault row is DIV0 unless the row says otherwise
  546:     s = Sess()
  547:     if not s.wait_prompt(0, 60):
  548:         s.close(); return dict(result="NOBOOT")
  549:     vbr = VBR_OF.get(name, 0)
  550:     entry = (vbr + 8 * vector) if vector is not None else None
  551:     words = [MARK] + prog + [SELF]
  552:     for i, w in enumerate(words):
  553:         s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * i, w))
  554:     for base in sorted({0, vbr}):                       # every entry spins unless a handler is planted (both tables if relocated)
  555:         for v in VECTORS:
  556:             s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (base + 8 * v, SELF))
  557:     if entry is not None:
  558:         s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (entry, SELF))
  559:     if handler:
  560:         for i, w in enumerate(handler):
  561:             s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (8 * handler_at + 4 * i, w))
  562:         s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (8 * handler_at + 4 * len(handler), SELF))
  563:     s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (DATA, 0x0badf00d))
  564:     for r, v in regs.items():
  565:         s.send("%s=0x%x" % (r, v))
  566:     s.send("r10=0x12345678"); s.send("r2=0xdeadbeef")
  567:     if vector is not None:
  568:         s.send("breakpoint add 0x%x" % entry)
  569:     s.send("pc=0x%x" % CODE)
  570:     mark = len(s.buf)
  571:     os.write(s.fd, b"continue\n")
  572:     t = time.time()
  573:     while time.time() - t < grace:
  574:         s.rd(0.3)
  575:         if s.buf[mark:].rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
  576:             break
  577:     if not s.buf[mark:].rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
  578:         os.write(s.fd, b"\x03"); s.wait_prompt(len(s.buf), 15)
  579:     tr = s.buf[mark:]
  580:     #  HALT is the fallback's diagnostic AND the machine-stopped observation (codex, pass 3: a mutant that keeps the
  581:     #  message and drops the stop would otherwise classify as HALT); an expected-HALT row must also see ITS vector named.
  582:     diag = re.search(r"unimplemented[^\r\n]*vector=0x([0-9a-f]+)", tr)
  583:     stopped = "machines stopped" in tr           # emul.c's `All machines stopped.' -- the ONE stop observation (pass 4:
  584:     halted = diag is not None and stopped        # an `or "stopped" in tr' disjunct had made the exact term dead code)
  585:     if halted and name in EXPECT_VECTOR and int(diag.group(1), 16) != EXPECT_VECTOR[name]:
  586:         halted = False                              # halted on some OTHER vector: not the row's answer
  587:     #  LEVEL channel (Fable review seat, pass 2): main.c lowers every subsystem to WARNING after startup, so a
  588:     #  free-running `continue' prints an ERROR-level `[ exception <CLASS>: ]' line and stays silent at INFO.  A
  589:     #  delivered row that carries the line has the pre-fix level (M_LEVEL) -- #261's opt-in break-on-ERROR would
  590:     #  then stop the emulator into the debugger on a user process's divide by zero.
  591:     levelmsg = re.search(r"exception (MISALIGNED_ACCESS|UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE|PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION|"
  592:                          r"BOUNDS_CHECK_VIOLATION|ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE|INTEGER_OVERFLOW|ERROR)", tr) is not None
  593:     tailhalt = "internal error" in tr

  596:     s.close()
  597:     started = (r9 == 0x12345678)
  598:     res = dict(pc=pc, sxip=sxip, snip=snip, sfip=sfip, psr=psr, epsr=epsr, r2=r2, transcript=tr)
  599:     if not started:
  600:         res["result"] = "NOSTART"; return res
  601:     if vector is None:
  602:         ok = (not stopped) and r2 == EXPECT_R2[name]
  603:         res["result"] = "DONE" if ok else ("STOP" if stopped else "BADREGS"); return res
  604:     if halted:

  606:         if name == "sfu1h" and pc != 8 * V_DIV0:

  608:     elif tailhalt:

  610:     elif stopped:
  611:         res["result"] = "STOP"
  612:     elif pc != entry:
  613:         res["result"] = "STOP"          # ran past its grace without the entry breakpoint firing
  614:     else:

  621:             # `trapfrozen' the second fault is a TRAP, which the manual (:8633-8636, 6.10 :10613-10614) exempts from

  641: def selftest():
  642:     s = Sess()
  643:     ok = s.wait_prompt(0, 60)
  644:     psr = s.cr("PSR") if ok else None

  648: def main():
  649:     ok, psr = selftest()

  656:         print("M88KEXC_ROW=%-9s RESULT=%-8s pc=%s sxip=%s snip=%s sfip=%s psr=%s epsr=%s r2=%s%s" % (
  657:             name, res.get("result"), fmt(res.get("pc")), fmt(res.get("sxip")), fmt(res.get("snip")),
  658:             fmt(res.get("sfip")), fmt(res.get("psr")), fmt(res.get("epsr")), fmt(res.get("r2")),
  659:             (" bad=" + ",".join(res["bad"])) if res.get("bad") else ""), flush=True)
  660:         if vector is not None:
  661:             total += 1
  662:             if res.get("result") == EXPECT_RESULT.get(name, "DELIVER"):
  663:                 delivered += 1
  664:         elif res.get("result") != "DONE":
  665:             controls_bad.append(name)
  666:         if os.environ.get("M88KEXC_VERBOSE"):
  667:             print("---- transcript ----"); print(res.get("transcript", "")[-1200:]); print("--------------------")
  668:     print("M88KEXC_RESULT=%d/%d" % (delivered, total), flush=True)
  669:     if controls_bad:
  670:         print("M88KEXC_CONTROLS=BAD %s" % ",".join(controls_bad), flush=True)
  671:     print("M88KEXC_DONE", flush=True)
  672:     #  exit status: the gate can rely on it (a row printing a failure word while the process exits 0 was a pass-2 finding)
  673:     sys.exit(0 if (ok and delivered == total and not controls_bad) else 1)

  223: PSR_EXC = 0x8000000b            # MODE | SFD1 | IND | SFRZ after the common path
  224: PSR_USER = 0x00000002           # IND only: what the user-mode rows write via stcr
  225: PSR_FRZSUP = 0x80000003         # MODE | IND | SFRZ, SFD1 CLEAR: a frozen handler that wants its divu to raise DIV0, not SFU1
  226: PSR_FRZUSER = 0x00000003        # IND | SFRZ: a frozen handler dropping to user mode (PRIVILEGE / UNIMPLEMENTED routes).
  227:                                 # *** MODE **AND SFD1** ARE BOTH CLEAR HERE, and this comment said only MODE until v20.
  228:                                 # That omission cost a prediction: PRETABLE15 expected M_RRTRAPSFD1 to red trapfrozensfd1

  230:                                 # presents SFD1 clear as well.  A constant's comment that lists SOME of the bits it clears
  231:                                 # reads like a complete decode and is not one.

  233: PSR_FRZNOIND = 0x80000009       # MODE | SFD1 | SFRZ, IND CLEAR: a frozen handler that re-enables interrupts (grok, pass 7)
  234: PSR_FRZC = 0x9000000b           # C | MODE | SFD1 | IND | SFRZ: a frozen handler with the carry bit set (grok, pass 7)
  235: PSR_SUPCNOIND = 0xb0000000      # SER | MODE | C, IND CLEAR: supervisor, carry set, interrupts enabled, SERIAL MODE set.  ONE value
  236:                                 # closes the C, IND and SER cells at an arm together (grok, pass 9: v16 set only MODE|C here and closed
  237:                                 # SER at ONE arm, which is the cell-not-class error inside the repair for that error).  ONE value closes BOTH
  238:                                 # open cells at a switch-1 arm, because the open cells are always (C never set) and (IND never clear)
  239:                                 # -- opus5's census, pass 8.  The same trick ovfc and indclr already play at the OVERFLOW arm.
  240: PSR_FRZSER = 0xa000000b         # SER | MODE | SFD1 | IND | SFRZ: a frozen handler with SERIAL MODE set.  SER is NOT an 88110-only bit:
  241: PSR_SERSUP = 0xa0000002         # SER | MODE | IND: supervisor with serial mode set and nothing else unusual -- the pre-fault value for
  242:                                 # the fallback and non-trap rows that close SER at those two sites (grok, pass 9)
  243:                                 # mc88100.txt, anchor `SER - Serial Mode', says "This bit is set by software as appropriate", and
  244:                                 # m88k_stcr stores PSR verbatim after its BO / MXM / MODE checks.  The fork's vendored header comments

  246:                                 # (codex, pass 8; checked against the manual on disk rather than against the header).

  248: def tri(sub, d, s1, s2):
  249:     return 0xf4000000 | (d << 21) | (s1 << 16) | (sub << 8) | s2

  267:                                                  #   PRIVILEGE with is_trap == 0 (cpu_m88k_instr.c X(tb0)), so privtb is NOT the trap route
  268: TB1_0_R3_450 = 0xf003d9c2                        # tb1 0,r3,450 -- the 0x1c2 syscall vector (OpenBSD >= 5.3): a switch-1 label of its own
  269: TB1_0_R3_496 = 0xf003d9f0                        # tb1 0,r3,496 -- MVMEPROM_VECTOR: likewise
