    2: """lunaabsentcpu (#503) -- rung-3 WITNESS and DETECTOR for dev_luna88k.c reassert_interrupts().
    3: 
    4: THE DEFECT.  reassert_interrupts() walked all MAX_CPUS (4) slots and called INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->cpu_irq[cpu]) --
    5: a direct call through the struct's function pointer -- while DEVINIT connected cpu_irq[1..3] only under
    6: `ncpus >= 2/3/4' and memset()s the device to zero.  The INT_ST_MASK0-3 and SOFT_INT0-3 store arms take their slot
    7: from the ADDRESS (`cpunr = (addr - INT_ST_MASK0) / 4') with no ncpus bound, so on the default ONE-cpu luna-88k a
    8: guest word store of 0xfc000000 to INT_ST_MASK1 (0x65000004) arms slot 1, and a following store of 1 to SOFT_INT1
    9: (0x69000004) sets that slot's pending bit and calls reassert SYNCHRONOUSLY inside the store: a NULL function
   10: pointer is called and the emulator process dies with SIGSEGV (MEASURED 2026-09-05: signal 11, no diagnostic).
   11: Any device line whose bit the armed slot's mask enables kills the same way on a running guest -- the clock is line
   12: 6 (bit 31); the SIO TX-interrupt route (line 5, bit 30) is SYNCHRONOUS and guest-drivable while stepping (session A2).
   13: 
   14: THE FIX (#503, option (a) of the pass-1 set): `d->ncpus' captured at DEVINIT and the walk bounded `cpu < d->ncpus'.
   15: The absent slots' registers stay guest-writable storage with readback (a MODEL question filed as lunaabsentmaskdecl).
   16:

   58:                                                      software interrupt pending): a DEVICE line must deliver to a CONNECTED
   59:                                                      slot -- cpu 0 enters VBR+8.  v2 had no such row (deepseek, grok, pass 2)

   63:                                                      (the first arrival is parked off the vector, then `continue' again) (kimi)

   66:                                                      walk DEASSERTS a still-pending IPI): a MASK0 rewrite after delivery keeps the

   70:                                                      -> a second SOFT_INT0 store is never delivered (deepseek, kimi, grok)
   71:   session C3 (`-n 2', FREE-RUNNING)  cpu 1 delivered, then cpu 0 reads SOFT_INT1 (clears it): slot 1 must DEASSERT

   73:                                                      tests slot 0's latch, which is 0 here -> no DEASSERT -> cpu 1 re-enters) (deepseek)
   74:   session E  (`-n 4', FREE-RUNNING)  all four cpus with VBR/PSR set and parked; MASKn bit 26 + SOFT_INTn for n = 0..3 from cpu 0
   75:   session E2 (`-n 4', FREE-RUNNING)  slots 2 and 3 pending, slots 0 and 1 IDLE -- the alias targets DISAGREE

   78:                                                   slot 2 aliases onto the IDLE slot 0 and slot 3 onto the IDLE slot 1, so the

   80:                                                   them -- it writes the SAME mask bit and the same IPI on every slot, so a

   91: RESULT words per row: OK / HALT (the host died: signal / exit) / BADREGS (a value assert failed) / STOP (no vector entry

  135: op26=0x09 ; br . = c0000000.
  136: usage: luna_absentcpu_probe.py <gxemul-binary> <images-dir>
  137: """
  138: import os, pty, re, select, sys, time
  139: 
  140: BIN     = sys.argv[1]
  141: IMAGES  = sys.argv[2]
  142: IMG     = IMAGES + "/liveimage-luna88k-raw-20250518.img"
  143: CODE    = 0x00010000
  144: SCRATCH = 0x00020000
  145: PARK    = 0x00030000        # `br .' for a parked cpu
  146: VBR     = 0x00040000        # a vector table in RAM: `br .' at VBR+8 (INTERRUPT)
  147: MASK0   = 0x65000000        # INT_ST_MASK0 -- the LITERAL, deliberately not the #define
  148: SOFT0   = 0x69000000        # SOFT_INT0
  149: SIOCMD0 = 0x51000004        # OBIO_SIO + 4: SCC command register, channel 0
  150: SELF    = 0xc0000000        # br .
  151: 
  152: 
  153: def enc(is_store, d, s1, imm16):
  154:     return ((0x09 if is_store else 0x05) << 26) | (d << 21) | (s1 << 16) | imm16
  155: 
  156: 
  157: SLOTS = [(CODE + 0x00, enc(True,  4, 5, 0), "st\tr4,r5,0x0"),
  158:          (CODE + 0x04, enc(False, 4, 5, 0), "ld\tr4,r5,0x0"),
  159:          (CODE + 0x08, enc(True,  4, 6, 0), "st\tr4,r6,0x0"),
  160:          (CODE + 0x0c, enc(False, 4, 6, 0), "ld\tr4,r6,0x0")]
  161: A_WDEV, A_RDEV, A_WRAM, A_RRAM = [s[0] for s in SLOTS]
  162: 
  163: rows = []
  164: fails = []
  165: t0 = time.time()
  166: 
  167: 
  168: def row(name, result, note=""):
  169:     rows.append((name, result))
  170:     if result != "OK":
  171:         fails.append(name)
  172:     print("LUNAABS_ROW=%-14s RESULT=%-8s %s" % (name, result, note), flush=True)
  173: 
  174: 
  175: class Sess:
  176:     def __init__(self, ncpus=1):
  177:         self.buf = ""
  178:         self.dead = False
  179:         self.status = None
  180:         argv = [BIN, "-V"] + (["-n", str(ncpus)] if ncpus > 1 else []) + ["-e", "luna-88k", "-d", "R:" + IMG, "boot"]
  181:         self.pid, self.fd = pty.fork()
  182:         if self.pid == 0:
  183:             os.chdir(IMAGES)
  184:             os.execvp(BIN, argv)
  185:             os._exit(127)
  186: 
  187:     def rd(self, t=0.4):
  188:         r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
  189:         if self.fd not in r:
  190:             return True
  191:         try:
  192:             d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
  193:         except OSError:
  194:             self.dead = True
  195:             return False
  196:         if not d:
  197:             self.dead = True
  198:             return False
  199:         self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
  200:         return True
  201: 
  202:     def wait(self, mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
  203:         t = time.time()
  204:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
  205:             if not self.rd():
  206:                 return False
  207:             resp = self.buf[mark:]
  208:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
  209:                 continue
  210:             if len(self.buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
  211:                 return True
  212:         return False
  213: 
  214:     def send(self, x, timeout=60):
  215:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
  216:         mark = len(self.buf)
  217:         n = 0
  218:         try:
  219:             while n < len(b):
  220:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
  221:         except OSError:
  222:             self.dead = True
  223:             return False
  224:         return self.wait(mark=mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
  225: 
  226:     def reg(self, name, cpu=None, cr=False):
  227:         """Read GPR/pc (`reg [cpu]') or a control register (`reg [cpu],c')."""
  228:         mark = len(self.buf)
  229:         cmd = "reg"
  230:         if cpu is not None:
  231:             cmd += " %d" % cpu
  232:         if cr:
  233:             cmd += ",c"
  234:         if not self.send(cmd, timeout=30):
  235:             return None
  236:         m = re.search(r"\b%s\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)" % re.escape(name), self.buf[mark:])
  237:         return int(m.group(1), 16) if m else None
  238: 
  239:     def step_at(self, addr):
  240:         if self.dead:
  241:             return False
  242:         if not self.send("pc=0x%08x" % addr):
  243:             return False
  244:         ok = self.send("step 1", timeout=30)
  245:         return ok and not self.dead

  392: 			    v & PG_U1?   "U1 " : "!u1",
  393: 			    v & PG_U0?   "U0 " : "!u0",
  394: 			    v & PG_SO?   "SP " : "!sp",
  395: 			    v & PG_M?    "M "  : "!m",
  396: 			    v & PG_U?    "U "  : "!u",
  397: 			    v & PG_PROT? "WP " : "!wp",
  398: 			    v & PG_V?    "V "  : "!v");
  399: 
  400: 			if (i == cmmu->patc_update_index)
  401: 				printf(" <--");
  402: 			printf("\n");
  403: 		}
  404: 	}
  405: }
  406: 
  407: 
  408: /*
  409:  *  m88k_irq_interrupt_assert():
  410:  *  m88k_irq_interrupt_deassert():
  411:  */
  412: void m88k_irq_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
  413: {
  414: 	struct cpu *cpu = (struct cpu *) interrupt->extra;
  415: 	cpu->cd.m88k.irq_asserted = 1;
  416: }
  417: void m88k_irq_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
  418: {
  419: 	struct cpu *cpu = (struct cpu *) interrupt->extra;
  420: 	cpu->cd.m88k.irq_asserted = 0;
  421: }
  422: 
  423: 
  424: /*
  425:  *  m88k_ldcr():
  426:  *
  427:  *  Read from a control register. Store the resulting value in a register
  428:  *  (pointed to by r32ptr).
  429:  */
  430: void m88k_ldcr(struct cpu *cpu, uint32_t *r32ptr, int cr)
  431: {
  432: 	uint32_t retval = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[cr];
  433: 
  434: 	switch (cr) {
  435: 
  436: 	case M88K_CR_PID:
  437: 	case M88K_CR_PSR:
  438: 	case M88K_CR_EPSR:
  439: 	case M88K_CR_SSBR:
  440: 	case M88K_CR_SXIP:
  441: 	case M88K_CR_SNIP:
  442: 	case M88K_CR_SFIP:
  443: 	case M88K_CR_VBR:
  444: 	case M88K_CR_DMD0:
  445: 	case M88K_CR_DMD1:
  446: 	case M88K_CR_DMD2:
  447: 	case M88K_CR_SR0:
  448: 	case M88K_CR_SR1:
  449: 	case M88K_CR_SR2:
  450: 	case M88K_CR_SR3:
  451: 		break;
  452: 
  453: 	case M88K_CR_DMT0:
  454: 	case M88K_CR_DMT1:
  455: 	case M88K_CR_DMT2:
  456: 		/*
  457: 		 *  Catch some possible internal errors in the emulator:
  458: 		 *
  459: 		 *  For valid memory Load transactions, the Destination Register
  460: 		 *  should not be zero.
  461: 		 *
  462: 		 *  The Byte Order bit should be the same as the CPU's.
  463: 		 */
  464: 		if (retval & DMT_VALID && !(retval & DMT_WRITE)) {
  465: 			if (DMT_DREGBITS(retval) == M88K_ZERO_REG) {
  466: 				debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
  467: 				    "ldcr: DMT DREG = zero? Internal error.");
  468: 				cpu->running = false;
  469: 				return;
  470: 			}
  471: 		}
  472: 		if (!!(cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_PSR] & M88K_PSR_BO)
  473: 		    != !!(retval & DMT_BO) && retval & DMT_VALID) {
  474: 			debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
  475: 			    "ldcr: DMT byte order not same as CPUs?");
  476: 			cpu->running = false;
  477: 			return;
  478: 		}
  479: 
  480: 		break;
  481: 
  482: 	case M88K_CR_DMA0:
  483: 	case M88K_CR_DMA1:
  484: 	case M88K_CR_DMA2:
  485: 		/*
  486: 		 *  Catch some possible internal errors in the emulator:
  487: 		 *  The lowest 2 bits of the transaction address registers
  488: 		 *  should always be zero.
  489: 		 */
  490: 		if (retval & 3) {
  491: 			debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
  492: 			    "ldcr: DMAx not word-aligned? Internal error.");
  493: 			cpu->running = false;
  494: 			return;
  495: 		}
  496: 
  497: 		break;
  498: 
  499: 	default:debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
  500: 		    "ldcr: UNIMPLEMENTED cr = 0x%02x (%s)\n",
  501: 		    cr, m88k_cr_name(cpu, cr));
  502: 		cpu->running = false;
  503: 		return;
  504: 	}
  505: 
  506: 	*r32ptr = retval;
  507: }
  508: 
  509: 
  510: /*
  511:  *  m88k_stcr():
  512:  *
  513:  *  Write to a control register.
  514:  *  (Used by both the stcr and rte instructions.)
  515:  */
  516: void m88k_stcr(struct cpu *cpu, uint32_t value, int cr, int rte)
  517: {
  518: 	uint32_t old = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[cr];
  519: 
  520: 	switch (cr) {
  521: 
  522: 	case M88K_CR_PSR:	/*  Processor Status Register  */
  523: 		if ((cpu->byte_order == EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN
  524: 		    && !(value & M88K_PSR_BO)) ||
  525: 		    (cpu->byte_order == EMUL_BIG_ENDIAN
  526: 		    && (value & M88K_PSR_BO))) {
  527: 			debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
  528: 			    "stcr: TODO: attempt to change endianness by flipping"
  529: 			    " the endianness bit in the PSR. How should this"
  530: 			    " be handled? Aborting.");
  531: 			cpu->running = false;
  532: 			return;
  533: 		}
  534: 
  535: 		if (!rte && old & M88K_PSR_MODE && !(value & M88K_PSR_MODE))
  536: 			debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_WARNING,
  537: 			    "stcr: WARNING! the PSR_MODE bit is being"
  538: 			    " cleared; this should be done using the RTE "
  539: 			    "instruction only, according to the M88100 "
  540: 			    "manual! Continuing anyway.");
  541: 
  542: 		if (value & M88K_PSR_MXM) {
  543: 			debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
  544: 			    "stcr: TODO: MXM support");
  545: 			cpu->running = false;
  546: 			return;
  547: 		}
  548: 
  549: 		if ((old & M88K_PSR_MODE) != (value & M88K_PSR_MODE))
  550: 			cpu->invalidate_translation_caches(
  551: 			    cpu, 0, INVALIDATE_ALL);
  552: 
  553: 		cpu->cd.m88k.cr[cr] = value;
  554: 		break;
  555: 
  556: 	case M88K_CR_EPSR:
  557: 		cpu->cd.m88k.cr[cr] = value;
  558: 		break;
  559: 
  560: 	case M88K_CR_SXIP:
  561: 	case M88K_CR_SNIP:
  562: 	case M88K_CR_SFIP:
  563: 		cpu->cd.m88k.cr[cr] = value;
  564: 		break;
  565: 
  566: 	case M88K_CR_SSBR:	/*  Shadow ScoreBoard Register  */
  567: 		if (value & 1)
  568: 			debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_WARNING,
  569: 			    "stcr: WARNING! bit 0 non-zero when"
  570: 			    " writing to SSBR (?)");
  571: 
  572: 		cpu->cd.m88k.cr[cr] = value;
  573: 		break;
  574: 
  575: 	case M88K_CR_VBR:
  576: 		if (value & 0x00000fff)
  577: 			debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_WARNING,
  578: 			    "stcr: WARNING! bits 0..11 non-zero when"
  579: 			    " writing to VBR (?)");
  580: 
  581: 		cpu->cd.m88k.cr[cr] = value;
  582: 		break;
  583: 
  584: 	case M88K_CR_DMT0:
  585: 	case M88K_CR_DMT1:
  586: 	case M88K_CR_DMT2:
  587: 		cpu->cd.m88k.cr[cr] = value;
  588: 		break;
  589: 
  590: 	case M88K_CR_SR0:	/*  Supervisor Storage Registers 0..3  */
  591: 	case M88K_CR_SR1:
  592: 	case M88K_CR_SR2:
  593: 	case M88K_CR_SR3:
  594: 		cpu->cd.m88k.cr[cr] = value;
  595: 		break;

  666:     s = boot(4)

 succeeded in 357ms:
 1300: > The detector passed (a), (b) and (c) and was still blind to a ONE-CHARACTER edit that
 1301: > re-opened the very defect the round had just closed. (b) restores the DEFECT; (d) neuters
 1302: > the FIX. A detector can be sensitive to one and blind to the other, because they fail at
 1303: > different sites: (b) fails where the old code was, (d) fails where the new code is.
 1304: >
 1305: > **HOW TO BUILD THE (d) MUTANT �?" and it is the same discriminator the accept-row rule
 1306: > already uses.** Take each predicate the fix **DEPENDS ON** and weaken it to the
 1307: > least-restrictive form that still *reads* like the same check: a mask narrowed
 1308: > (`& 3` -> `& 1`), a condition qualified (`if (X)` -> `if (X && writeflag == MEM_WRITE)`),
 1309: > a bound relaxed. **Not deletion** �?" deletion is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes and is usually
 1310: > already covered. The test is whether a plausible future edit could produce it.
 1311: >
 1312: > **WHY THE ONE-CHARACTER FORM IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** `dma alias`, the row written
 1313: > specifically to pin that repair, was **STRUCTURALLY BLIND** to it: it used an ODD address,
 1314: > which both guard forms drop. *The row aimed at the defect could not see the defect return.*
 1315: > A row written from the FIX's intent tests the intent, not the boundary �?" so (d)'s mutant
 1316: > has to come from an adversary, which is exactly what the ask-again buys.
 1317: >
 1318: > **COST: one mutant per repair predicate**, the same order as the accept-row rule (#448 = 4,
 1319: > #449 = 1, #461 = 4 across two arms). It is bounded by the diff, so it cannot become #392:
 1320: > the predicates are the ones the fix ADDED, and that list is finite and visible.
 1321: >
 1322: > ### *** "THE PREDICATE THE FIX INTRODUCED" ENUMERATED THE EMPTY SET FOR MOST OF THIS
 1323: > ### QUEUE, AND NOBODY NOTICED FOR THREE ROUNDS. Corrected 2026-08-24 -- clause (d) now
 1324: > ### reads DEPENDS ON, not INTRODUCED. ***
 1325: >
 1326: > Clause (d) says *keep the guard, null its repair*. As written it enumerated only
 1327: > predicates the diff ADDS -- and **the two commonest fix shapes here add none**:
 1328: >
 1329: > * a **pure deletion** introduces no predicate at all;
 1330: > * a **DE-FATALISATION** introduces none either -- it changes what an EXISTING
 1331: >   predicate's branch does. That is most of this project's queue.
 1332: >
 1333: > So for those rounds clause (d) enumerated **nothing**, and a round could satisfy the
 1334: > floor while having built no (d) mutant whatever. `#461` measured (d) successfully only
 1335: > because its fix happened to ADD a predicate (an alignment mask) for the mutant to weaken.
 1336: >
 1337: > **Reading it as DEPENDS ON fixes both cases at once**, and it is why this now replaces a
 1338: > narrower deletion-only rule written earlier the same day: that rule was a special case of
 1339: > this gap, and two overlapping statements of one rule is the accumulation this carrier is
 1340: > supposed to refuse.
 1341: >
 1342: > **The two shapes, once the enumeration is right:**
 1343: > * **fix ADDS a predicate** -> weaken the added one (unchanged; the original rule).
 1344: > * **fix adds none** -> the (d) mutant reroutes an EXISTING guard's repair. For a
 1345: >   de-fatalisation: **swap the guard's operand for a field the guarded BODY reads.**
 1346: >   For a pure deletion: **re-introduce the removed behaviour under a DIFFERENT predicate**
 1347: >   -- not the same one widened, which is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes.
 1348: >
 1349: > *** THE MEASURED CASE, and it is why this matters rather than being tidy: ***
 1350: > `#462` de-fatalised two arms. Its guard reads `d->data`; the body it guards reads
 1351: > `d->data` AND `d->data_len`; the two are set together in one place and cleared in two
 1352: > places that touch only ONE of them. Swapping the guard's operand to `d->data_len == 0`
 1353: > **scored a CLEAN SWEEP on a 52-row detector that had just passed twelve mutants**, and
 1354: > restored a NULL-pointer read of 408 bytes. Under the old wording that was coverage debt;
 1355: > under DEPENDS ON it is a **floor breach, L2, fix in-round** -- which is what the round
 1356: > did, before the wording was corrected.
 1357: >
 1358: > #### *** THE BOUND, and it is not optional: "DEPENDS ON" INVITES TRANSITIVE DATA-FLOW
 1359: > #### CHASING, which is unbounded and is how this becomes `#392`. Named 2026-08-24 by the
 1360: > #### adjudicating seat, in the same breath as upholding the correction. ***
 1361: >
 1362: > > **DEPENDS ON enumerates exactly two things: (1) the predicates DOMINATING the diff's
 1363: > > edited lines, walked up only to the ENCLOSING FUNCTION BOUNDARY -- never into callees
 1364: > > or callers; and (2) for operand-swap forms, swaps drawn ONLY from fields the guarded
 1365: > > BODY reads (the guard-field/body-field grep the pre-table check already mandates).**
 1366: >
 1367: > That is finite, derivable from the diff by reading, and **it provably contains the
 1368: > measured case**: the `d->data == NULL` guard dominates the edited decline (clause 1), and
 1369: > `data_len` is a body-read field (clause 2), so `M11` falls out mechanically rather than
 1370: > by inspiration. It cannot become `#392` because the enumeration stays closed over the
 1371: > diff's own hunks -- the same closure the INTRODUCED wording had, without the empty-set
 1372: > defect that made INTRODUCED enumerate nothing for a de-fatalisation.
 1373: >
 1374: > #### *** JOINT CELLS -- adjudicated 2026-09-04 on `pcc2widthkills` pass 10 by the flagship seat, after
 1375: > #### a measuring seat's CONJUNCTION of two body-read counts escaped 190/190 AND THE SAME SEAT ARGUED IT
 1376: > #### WAS NOT A BREACH.  Both proposed bounds were rejected; the one adopted came from neither. ***
 1377: >
 1378: > > **DEPENDS-ON enumerates JOINT cells over the guard's operands and the body-read lvalues EXACTLY
 1379: > > WHEN THE GUARDED BODY'S OWN CODE DISTINGUISHES THEM.**  For each lvalue take the PARTITION the
 1380: > > body's own tests induce (`pending > 0` gives {0, >0}; `relative_addr == PCCTWO_T1ICR` gives
 1381: > > {T1, T2}); the enumerated cells are the CROSS-PRODUCT of those partitions -- never arbitrary
 1382: > > boolean functions of the read-set.  A mutant exempting a single cell whose reachability is
 1383: > > MEASURED is a floor breach; close the whole cross-product in ONE diff and write into the
 1384: > > contract which cells are rowed.  A value or combination the body never tests as a case
 1385: > > (`idata == k`; a byte no conditional distinguishes; both counts >= 2 where the body tests `> 0`)
 1386: > > is DISCLOSED under clause (e).
 1387: >
 1388: > **Why this form and not the two that were argued.**  The dissent's discriminator -- *a plausible
 1389: > future edit* -- failed three ways: plausibility is not derivable by reading; the mutant was written
 1390: > and measured, so it is an edit someone writes; and "single-operand is complete" is measurably
 1391: > false, since `&& idata != 2` is single-operand and escapes.  The main loop's own bound -- *pairs of
 1392: > body-read lvalues over a finite reachable space* -- was rejected as SELF-REFUTING: the counts' raw
 1393: > value space is 2^32 apiece, so "finite" does no bounding work on its motivating case (a C `int` is
 1394: > finite -- the first wording here said it was not; codex and glm caught it on pass 11); "reachable
 1395: > guest state" would force rowing
 1396: > `idata == 2` that the same record discloses; and a PAIRS cap misses `#499`'s four axes.  The
 1397: > body-tested-partition form is finite (a product of small partition counts), derivable from the
 1398: > diff by reading the body's own `if`s, and it enumerates `#499`'s width x component x direction x
 1399: > position cells from `len`, `writeflag` and `relative_addr` -- which neither argued bound reached.
 1400: >
 1401: > **The measured case.**  The TxICR body tests both pending counts at `> 0` and the label at
 1402: > `== T1ICR` / `T2ICR`, so the closure is {T2, T1} x {p1 = 0, > 0} x {p2 = 0, > 0} -- eight cells.
 1403: > Pass 9 held one open cell (both counts, T2 label: a 190/190 escape, predicted by the Opus seat
 1404: > before its build); pass 10 found the MIRROR (both counts, T1 label) and the (p2 only, T1 label)
 1405: > cell open -- the `#499` shape reproduced inside the repair for it -- and the label x p1 x p2 x IEN
 1406: > product (sixteen cells) closed in one diff of nine readbacks in existing sessions; the ICLR, INT and
 1407: > 0xe0 partitions were disclosed by count, and pass 11 then found the ICLR x pending cell open and
 1408: > in-rule (this paragraph said "eight cells ... three readbacks" until pass 11).  **The De Morgan form of the gap**, from the kimi
 1409: > substitute: `!(p1 && p2)` is `p1 == 0 || p2 == 0`; neither disjunct is true of all its lvalue's
 1410: > held values, the disjunction is true in every row, so a per-lvalue disclosure test can never fire
 1411: > on it.  That is why the per-lvalue value-domain statement, correct as far as it went, could not
 1412: > see this class at all.
 1413: >
 1414: > **Two corollaries from the same pass, both measured.**  The PRE-TABLE CHECK's SIBLING PARITY row
 1415: > compares BLOCKS; at a block that serves TWO case labels the siblings are the LABELS, and a
 1416: > parity census over the row names is what would have shown `pendboth` as the only T2-only cell
 1417: > before its table ran (Opus, pass 10).  And the measuring seat's own bound -- the cross-product
 1418: > of the value-CLASSES the rows establish, with the dispatch axes as full axes, demand
 1419: > INCREMENTAL -- agrees with the body-tested form on every cell that mattered here and differs on
 1420: > where the classes come from (the rows, or the body's tests); that residual difference is

  668:         row("cpu3 deliver23", "NOSTART"); row("cpu2 deliver23", "NOSTART"); row("others idle23", "NOSTART"); return
  669:     for c in (1, 2, 3):

  673:     #  cpu 1 (M_AND1 reads BADREGS with pcs[1] at the vector) and CANNOT see one onto cpu 0 -- the row's own
  674:     #  channel was half blind.  A CHANNEL filter on an existing row, not a new cell (grok, pass 5).

  682:     s.send("breakpoint add 0x%08x" % (VBR + 8))
  683:     entered = set()

  693:                 s.send("focus %d" % c); s.send("pc=0x%08x" % PARK); s.send("focus 0")      # park the arrival off the vector
  694:         if not hit or len(entered) == 2:

  698:     row("cpu2 deliver23", "OK" if 2 in entered else "STOP",

# DERIVER for the {switch-1 arm} x {settable PSR bit} cell census of the #502 detector.
#
# WHY THIS EXISTS: my pass-10 packet asserted "the previously-closed 23" with no deriver.  kimi could not
# reconstruct 23 from any artifact and derived 34 of 40 instead.  The carrier's rule is that a count names its
# deriver or carries its method inline; a count I cannot re-derive is exactly the class this round has already
# been bitten by twice today.  So: enumerate the cells mechanically, print the GRID (not the total), and let the
# total fall out of it.  A per-cell breakdown is checkable; a total is not.
#
# THE CELL DEFINITION, and it is the one thing here that is a judgement rather than a transcription:
#   a mutant at arm A conditioning `implemented' on bit B -- in EITHER polarity -- is killed iff two rows enter
#   switch 1 at arm A with DIFFERENT values of B.  So cell (A,B) is CLOSED iff bit B VARIES across the rows that
#   reach arm A.  That is polarity-free, which is why it is the right formulation: v16's comment observes that
#   the open cells "are always (C never set) and (IND never clear)", i.e. the arm's rows AGREE on the bit.
#
# THE ARM A ROW ENTERS IS NOT ITS EXPECTED VECTOR.  Every err*/trapfrozen* row faults TWICE: the first fault
# gets the handler running, the second is the one under test.  The re-route rows then have their vector
# REWRITTEN to ERROR after switch 1, so their expected vector (V_ERROR) names a place they never entered.
# Both faults are recorded below, each with the PSR in force at that moment, sourced from the row's own
# setup/handler in m88k_exc_probe.py.
MODE, SER, C, SFD1, IND, SFRZ = 0x80000000, 0x20000000, 0x10000000, 0x08, 0x02, 0x01
BITS = [("MODE", MODE), ("SER", SER), ("C", C), ("SFD1", SFD1), ("IND", IND), ("SFRZ", SFRZ)]
ARMS = ["MISALIGN", "UNIMPL", "PRIV", "BOUNDS", "DIV0", "OVF", "ERROR"]

RESET, USER, EXC = 0x80000002, 0x00000002, 0x8000000b
CARRY, INDCLR, SUPCNOIND, SERSUP = 0x90000002, 0x80000000, 0xb0000000, 0xa0000002
FRZSUP, FRZUSER, FRZNOIND, FRZC, FRZSER = 0x80000003, 0x00000003, 0x80000009, 0x9000000b, 0xa000000b

# (row, [(arm, psr_at_that_arm), ...])   -- second entry present only for the two-fault rows
FAULTS = {
 "div0": [("DIV0", RESET)], "ovf": [("OVF", RESET)], "misalign": [("MISALIGN", RESET)],
 "bounds": [("BOUNDS", RESET)], "priv": [("PRIV", USER)], "privtb": [("PRIV", USER)],
 "unimpl": [("UNIMPL", USER)], "dslot": [("DIV0", RESET)], "errortb": [("ERROR", RESET)],
 "misaligntb": [("MISALIGN", RESET)], "unimpltb": [("UNIMPL", RESET)], "div0tb": [("DIV0", RESET)],
 "ovftb": [("OVF", RESET)], "vbrtb": [("BOUNDS", RESET)], "privsuptb": [("PRIV", RESET)],
 "udiv0": [("DIV0", USER)], "ovfc": [("OVF", CARRY)], "indclr": [("OVF", INDCLR)],
 "umisalign": [("MISALIGN", USER)], "uovf": [("OVF", USER)],
 "error":     [("DIV0", RESET), ("MISALIGN", EXC)],
 "errovf":    [("DIV0", RESET), ("OVF", EXC)],
 "errind":    [("DIV0", RESET), ("MISALIGN", FRZNOIND)],
 "errc":      [("DIV0", RESET), ("MISALIGN", FRZC)],
 "errser":    [("DIV0", RESET), ("MISALIGN", FRZSER)],
 "errdiv0":   [("DIV0", RESET), ("DIV0", FRZSUP)],
 "errpriv":   [("DIV0", RESET), ("PRIV", FRZUSER)],
 "errunimpl": [("DIV0", RESET), ("UNIMPL", FRZUSER)],
 "trapfrozen":  [("DIV0", RESET), ("BOUNDS", EXC)],
 "trapfrozen4": [("DIV0", RESET), ("MISALIGN", EXC)],
 "trapfrozen5": [("DIV0", RESET), ("UNIMPL", EXC)],
 "trapfrozen6": [("DIV0", RESET), ("PRIV", EXC)],
 "trapfrozen8": [("OVF", RESET), ("DIV0", EXC)],
 "trapfrozen9": [("DIV0", RESET), ("OVF", EXC)],
 "trapfrozen10":[("DIV0", RESET), ("ERROR", EXC)],
 "trapfrozenind":[("DIV0", RESET), ("BOUNDS", FRZNOIND)],
 "trapfrozenc":  [("DIV0", RESET), ("BOUNDS", FRZC)],
 "trapfrozenser":[("DIV0", RESET), ("BOUNDS", FRZSER)],
 "unimpltbc": [("UNIMPL", SUPCNOIND)], "privtbc": [("PRIV", SUPCNOIND)],
 "boundstbc": [("BOUNDS", SUPCNOIND)], "div0tbc": [("DIV0", SUPCNOIND)],
 "errortbc":  [("ERROR", SUPCNOIND)],  "ovftbser": [("OVF", SUPCNOIND)],
 # rows that reach no switch-1 arm under test: the fallback rows, the user-trap rows, the SFU1 rows.
 # sfu1h and the trapfrozen user-trap rows still fault FIRST at a real arm, so that fault counts.
 "sfu1h": [("DIV0", RESET)], "trapfrozen450": [("DIV0", RESET)], "trapfrozen496": [("DIV0", RESET)],
 "trapfrozen511": [("DIV0", RESET)], "trapfrozen128": [("DIV0", RESET)],
 "resid50": [], "resid127": [], "resid11": [], "resid50ind": [], "resid50c": [], "resid50ser": [],
 "usertb": [], "usfu1": [],
}
V16_ONLY = {"ovftbser", "resid50ser", "errser"}     # the three rows v17 ADDED
V16_PSR = {"unimpltbc": 0x90000000, "privtbc": 0x90000000, "boundstbc": 0x90000000,
           "div0tbc": 0x90000000, "errortbc": 0x90000000}   # SUPCNOIND before v17 ORed SER in

def census(rows, psr_override):
    seen = {}                       # arm -> bit -> set of values observed
    for r in rows:
        for (arm, psr) in FAULTS.get(r, []):
            if arm not in ARMS: continue
            psr = psr_override.get(r, psr) if len(FAULTS[r]) == 1 else psr
            for (bn, bv) in BITS:
                seen.setdefault(arm, {}).setdefault(bn, set()).add(1 if (psr & bv) else 0)
    return seen

ALL = [r for r in FAULTS]
def report(title, rows, override):
    s = census(rows, override)
    print("\n=== %s  (%d rows enumerated) ===" % (title, len(rows)))
    print("%-9s %s" % ("arm", "  ".join("%-5s" % b for b, _ in BITS)))
    closed = 0; openc = []
    for a in ARMS:
        cells = []
        for (bn, _) in BITS:
            vals = s.get(a, {}).get(bn, set())
            if len(vals) > 1: cells.append("%-5s" % "CLOSE"); closed += 1
            else:
                v = ("=1" if 1 in vals else "=0") if vals else "--"
                cells.append("%-5s" % ("open" + v)); openc.append((a, bn, v))
        print("%-9s %s" % (a, "  ".join(cells)))
    print("closed %d of %d cells; OPEN: %s" % (closed, len(ARMS) * len(BITS),
          ", ".join("%s x %s%s" % (a, b, v) for a, b, v in openc) or "none"))
    return closed, openc

report("v17/v18 -- the SHIPPED instrument (57 fault rows)", ALL, {})
report("v16 -- the instrument pass 9 reviewed", [r for r in ALL if r not in V16_ONLY], V16_PSR)

# ---- THE OTHER TWO SITES, because a pass-10 seat (minimax) asked the right question about one of them.
# It read `resid50ser''s description -- "the fallback, whose own blurb already claimed every settable bit on
# both sides" -- as a row whose blurb claims an axis while the row tests one cell, i.e. pass 9's defect shape
# reproduced.  That is exactly the challenge this brief asked for, and it is answerable by census rather than
# by argument: the claim is true of the ROW SET, not of any single row, which is grok's "the axis is the set
# of rows, not any one of them".  Printed here so the answer is measured instead of asserted.
FALLBACK = {"resid50": RESET, "resid127": RESET, "resid11": RESET, "resid50ind": INDCLR,
            "resid50c": CARRY, "resid50ser": SERSUP, "usfu1": 0x0000000a, "sfu1h": EXC}
RR_NONTRAP = {"error": EXC, "errovf": EXC, "errind": FRZNOIND, "errc": FRZC, "errser": FRZSER,
              "errdiv0": FRZSUP, "errpriv": FRZUSER, "errunimpl": FRZUSER}
RR_TRAP = {"trapfrozen": EXC, "trapfrozen4": EXC, "trapfrozen5": EXC, "trapfrozen6": EXC, "trapfrozen8": EXC,
           "trapfrozen9": EXC, "trapfrozen10": EXC, "trapfrozen128": EXC, "trapfrozen450": EXC,
           "trapfrozen496": EXC, "trapfrozen511": EXC,
           "trapfrozenind": FRZNOIND, "trapfrozenc": FRZC, "trapfrozenser": FRZSER}

def site(title, table, drop=()):
    t = {k: v for k, v in table.items() if k not in drop}
    print("\n=== %s  (%d rows) ===" % (title, len(t)))
    for (bn, bv) in BITS:
        vals = {(1 if (p & bv) else 0): [] for p in t.values()}
        for r, p in sorted(t.items()): vals[1 if (p & bv) else 0].append(r)
        if len(vals) > 1:
            odd = min(vals, key=lambda k: len(vals[k]))
            print("  %-5s CLOSED   %s=%d by %s" % (bn, bn, odd, ", ".join(vals[odd])))
        else:
            print("  %-5s open     every row has %s=%d" % (bn, bn, list(vals)[0]))

site("FALLBACK predicate -- v17/v18", FALLBACK)

THE FINDING BEING REPAIRED (pass 4; kimi and grok INDEPENDENTLY, on the same packet).  The walk's per-cpu READS can be aliased and
probe v5 cannot see it:
    uint32_t enable = d->interrupt_enable[cpu];      ->  d->interrupt_enable[cpu & 1]      (M_ENP1)

Both PREDICTED 34/34 on v5 -- a total escape -- by both seats, and NEITHER had been run when the prediction was made.  Reason: v5's
session E writes the SAME mask bit (bit 26) and the same SOFT_INTn value on ALL FOUR slots, so a parity alias on a READ still finds a
correctly-set partner; the parity alias v5 DOES kill (M_IRQACTIVEP1, measured 32/34 in table 4) is on a WRITE, which the first arrival
of each pair consumes.  Every other multi-slot session pends slots whose alias target agrees, or a lone slot 1 (identity under `& 1').
The v4->v5 rewrite REPLACED the session that had the discriminating configuration (v4's E: slots 2 and 3 pending, 0 and 1 IDLE) instead
of adding beside it -- so the repair for one cell of this class deleted the coverage of another.

GRADE: clause (d) floor breach (an operand swap drawn from a field the guarded body READS, on the predicate that dominates the walk's
body).  L2 -- repaired in-round.  Not debt.

THE REPAIR IS A CLASS CLOSURE, NOT A CELL.  Passes 3 and 4 both found breaches of the {slot} x {per-cpu array} alias class, so by the
class-closure amendment the repair enumerates the whole space from the function's own inputs and closes it in ONE diff.  The walk
touches four per-cpu arrays; a configuration in which every one of them has a DISAGREEING alias target closes all of them at once:

    array                          alias forms       killed by, after this diff
    interrupt_enable[cpu]          [cpu & 1], [0]    session E2 (NEW): 2->0 and 3->1 are both IDLE
    software_interrupt_status[cpu] [cpu & 1], [0]    session E2 (NEW)
    irqActive[cpu]                 [cpu & 1], [0]    session E (M_IRQACTIVEP1 32/34, M_IRQACTIVE0 29/34, table 4)
    cpu_irq[cpu]                   [cpu & 1]         session E (M_AND1 32/34, table 4)

DIFF UNDER THE TABLE (on top of v5, three rows added, none removed -- v5's session E is KEPT):
  session E2 (`-n 4', FREE-RUNNING): MASK2 + SOFT_INT2 and MASK3 + SOFT_INT3 only; MASK0/MASK1 left at their boot value.  Rows
  `cpu3 deliver23', `cpu2 deliver23' (both cpus must enter VBR+8) and `others idle23' (cpus 0 and 1 must NOT).  This is v4's session E
  restored verbatim in shape, with the row names suffixed so the v5 all-four rows keep theirs.  No product change.

SIBLING PARITY: every slot 0..3 now has (i) a single-pending delivery row, (ii) a delivery row under a four-pending machine (E), and
(iii) for slots 2 and 3, a delivery row under a machine where their alias targets are IDLE (E2).  Slots 0 and 1 need no E2 row: their
alias targets under `& 1' and `[0]' are themselves.

GUARD FIELD vs BODY FIELD: the disagreeing state is built explicitly -- two slots pending whose `& 1' and `[0]' targets are unpended.

CHANNEL per new row (mutant -> observable): cpu3 deliver23 and cpu2 deliver23 red under M_ENP1 / M_SWP1 / M_EN0 / M_SW0 as a
VECTOR-ENTRY difference (the cpu never reaches VBR+8, reported STOP with its pc), which is the same channel every other delivery row
uses and cannot be vacuous by construction: under the fix both cpus enter, under each mutant neither does.  `others idle23' is an
accept-side sibling and reds only on a mutant that delivers to an unpended slot.

import ast, operator, pathlib
p=pathlib.Path('regress/m88k_exc_probe.py')
t=ast.parse(p.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
vals={}
ops={ast.BitOr:operator.or_,ast.LShift:operator.lshift,ast.Add:operator.add}
def ev(n):
    if isinstance(n,ast.Constant): return n.value
    if isinstance(n,ast.Name): return vals[n.id]
    if isinstance(n,(ast.List,ast.Tuple)): return [ev(x) for x in n.elts]
    if isinstance(n,ast.Dict): return {ev(k):ev(v) for k,v in zip(n.keys,n.values)}
    if isinstance(n,ast.BinOp): return ops[type(n.op)](ev(n.left),ev(n.right))
    if isinstance(n,ast.Call) and isinstance(n.func,ast.Name) and n.func.id=='tri':
        sub,d,s1,s2=map(ev,n.args); return 0xf4000000|(d<<21)|(s1<<16)|(sub<<8)|s2
    raise ValueError(ast.dump(n))
def put(n,v):
    if isinstance(n,ast.Name): vals[n.id]=v
    else:
        for k,x in zip(n.elts,v): put(k,x)
for n in t.body:
    if isinstance(n,ast.Assign) and 219<=n.lineno<=446:
        v=ev(n.value)
        for k in n.targets: put(k,v)
MODE,SER,C,SFD1,IND,SFRZ=0x80000000,0x20000000,0x10000000,8,2,1
bits=[('MODE',MODE),('SER',SER),('C',C),('SFD1',SFD1),('IND',IND),('SFRZ',SFRZ)]
events=[]
# Decode only the fixed instruction forms present in the row declarations, without running a guest or probe.
def classify(w,regs,psr):
    op=w>>26
    d,s1,s2=(w>>21)&31,(w>>16)&31,w&31
    rv=lambda r: 0 if r==0 else regs.get('r%d'%r,0)
    if (w&0xfc00fc00)==0x80008000:
        assert ((w>>5)&63) in (1,7)
        return ('psr',rv(s1)) if ((w>>5)&63)==1 else None
    if op==5:
        assert (rv(s1)+(w&65535))%4
        return ('fault',4,False)
    if (w&0xfc00fc00)==0xf000d800:
        assert rv(s1)&(1<<d)
        v=w&511
        return ('fault',6,False) if v<128 and not psr&MODE else ('fault',v,True)
    if w==vals['PROM_CALL']:
        assert not psr&MODE
        return ('fault',5,False)
    if w==vals['LDCR_R2_CR1']:
        assert not psr&MODE
        return ('fault',6,False)
    if op==61 and ((w>>8)&255)==0x68:
        if psr&SFD1: return ('fault',114,False)
        if rv(s2)==0: return ('fault',8,False)
        return None
    if op==61 and ((w>>8)&255)==0x70:
        assert rv(s1)==0x7fffffff and rv(s2)==1
        return ('fault',9,False)
    if op==49: return None
    raise ValueError(hex(w))
for row in vals['ROWS']:
    name,prog,regs,vec,fpc,epsr,handler=row[:7]
    psr=vals['PSR_RESET']
    for stage,words in enumerate([prog]+([handler] if handler else [])):
        for w in words:
            x=classify(w,regs,psr)
            if not x: continue
            if x[0]=='psr': psr=x[1]; continue
            _,v,tr=x
            events.append((name,stage,v,tr,psr))
            psr|=MODE|SFD1|IND|SFRZ
            break

 1985: MODE - Supervisor/User Mode
 1986:       This bit is set by hardware when the processor changes to the supervisor mode (due
 1987:       to an exception condition or trap instruction); it may be cleared by software to return
 1988:       the MC88100 to operating in the user mode. Refer to 2.1.2.3 CHANGING LEVELS OF
 1989:       PRIVILEGE for more information related to the MODE bit.
 1990:          0- User Mode
 1991:          1 - Supervisor Mode
 1992: 
 1993:                                                                   NOTE
 1994: 
 1995:              The MODE bit should only be cleared by an rte instruction. When the MODE
 1996:              bit is cleared by a control register instruction (load or exchange), two prob-
 1997:              lems can occur. First, the privilege check on the next instruction may not be
 1998:              correct and second, the next instruction may have been fetched from the

10250: 
10251: 6.8.2 Floating-Point Exception Processing Effects
10252: 
10253: A bit is set in the FPECR whenever the MC88100 branches to a floating-point exception
10254: vector due to an exception condition. The MC88100 then checks the corresponding bit in
10255: the FPCR to see if a user handler is enabled. The relationship of the bits in these registers
10256: is defined by the IEEE 754 standard and is shown in Figure 6-8.
10257: 
10258: MOTOROLA      MC88100 USER'S MANUAL  6-39
10259:       FPECR                              FPCR, FPSR
10260: 
10261:       FIOV
10262: 
10263:       6 FUNIMP
10264: 
10265:       FPRV                               IE.AIFINV
10266:       FROP ----------:~~
10267: 
10268:       FDVZ -===----------i~              IE.AIFDVZ
10269:                                          IE.AIFUNV
10270:       FUNF - - - - - - - - - - - i.~. 2
10271: 
10272:       FOVF -----------i.~ 1 IE.AIFOVF
10273: 
10274:       FINX - - - - - - - - - - - . ,.~. 0 IE.AIFINX
10275: 
10276:       Figure 6-8. Floating-point Exception Register Bit Relationships
10277: 
10278: When a floating-point exception occurs, the FPU performs the following actions:
10279:    1. Signals the instruction unit that the exception occurred.
10280:    2. Sets the appropriate bit in the FPECR to indicate the exception type.
10281:   3. Sets the SFUD1 bit in the PSR, which disables the FPU and freezes the floating-point
10282:        execution pipelines. When the execution pipelines are frozen, the internal registers
10283:        are frozen. The information saved within the FPU as part of the context depends on
10284:        whether the exception is precise or imprecise.
10285:    4. The floating-point instruction that caused the exception is removed from the FPU
10286:        pipeline.
10287: 
10288: Once the instruction unit is signaled and recognizes the exception, the integer unit performs
10289: the standard exception processing. The exact cause of the exception is determined when
10290: the exception handler reads the FPECR.
10291: 
10292: When the FPU is frozen, the control registers can be read or written, but no floating-point
10293: instructions (except fldcr, fstcr, and fxcr) can be executed. However, only the instruction
10294: that caused the exception is affected by the exception; When the FPU is restarted (at the
10295: end of exception processing by an rte instruction), the instructions in the pipeline resume
10296: execution. The instruction that caused the exception must be completed (or discarded) by
10297: software. Scoreboard bits for destination registers of an instruction that causes a floating-
10298: point exception may be set, and the handler must clear them appropriately.
10299: 
10300: 6.8.3 Integer-Divide Error Exception
10301: 
10302: This exception occurs when the divisor of a div or divu instruction is zero or when either
10303: operand is negative in a div instruction. The SXIP points to the instruction that caused the
10304: exception. The destination register is not changed, but the scoreboard bit is set for the
10305: destination register.
10306: 
10307: 6-40            MC88100 USER'S MANUAL                                  MOTOROLA
10308: Integer divide instructions are performed by the FPU. When an integer-divide error excep-
10309: tion occurs, floating-point control registers fcr2, fcr4, and fcr5 contain information pertinent
10310: to the integer-divide error exception. Registers fcr2 and fcr4 contain the source operands,
10311: and fcr5 contains the destination register number. The source operands can be examined
10312: by the exception handler to determine the cause of the exception (zero divisor or negative
10313: operand). The data in fcr5 can be used to clear the destination register scoreboard bit. The
10314: opcode in fcr5 can not determine which instruction (div or divu) caused the exception.
10315: 
10316: 6.8.4 Floating-Point Precise Exceptions (Vector Offset $390)
10317: 
10318: The following paragraphs provide information on each of the conditions that cause a
10319: floating-point precise exception. In all of the cases, the scoreboard bit(s) for the destination
10320: register(s) are set before the exception is recognized. The exception handler must clear
10321: those bits as part of exception handling; since the instruction that caused the precise
10322: exception is not executed, no result will be written to the general-purpose registers.
10323: 
10324: 6.8.4.1 FPU DISABLED. This exception occurs when the FPU is disabled and an instruction
10325: is dispatched to the FPU. The unit is disabled when SFD1 (bit 3) bit of the PSR is set. The
10326: instructions executed by the FPU include all floating-point arithmetic and convert instruc-
10327: tions and the integer multiply and divide instructions.

10611: 6.10 ERROR EXCEPTION (VECTOR OFFSET $50)
10612: 
10613: The error exception occurs when shadowing is frozen (SFRZ bit set in PSR) and an exception
10614: other than a trap instruction occurs. When shadowing is frozen, the shadow registers do
10615: 
10616: MOTOROLA                               MC88100 USER'S MANUAL                                                       6-47
10617:     not contain valid data because they are not updated from the time that they were frozen.
10618:     Shadowing is only frozen by the MC881 00 when an exception occurs, so the error exception
10619:     usually occurs when the MC88100 has not finished processing a first exception and a
10620:     second exception occurs (catastrophic condition). The error exception provides the means
10621:     to terminate processing when catastrophic situations are encountered.
10622: 
10623:     Since the shadow registers are not updated when shadowing is frozen, the processor
10624:     context needed for recovering from an error exception is not available. Therefore, the
